## School study priority (2026-11-05; build 283 installed; native acceptance passed)

- Report from a Workshop commenter, relayed by the user: students developed hunger/thirst/exhaustion and died after the school was paused during work hours; pausing after hours reportedly did not cause deaths. The affected pre-death save and exact sequence have not been supplied, and the deaths are not reproduced.
- Confirmed source defect: all three study effects used `SatisfyToMaxValue: true`, making native attraction decisions return directly to studying before root behaviors can reconsider essential needs. The generator and regenerated school AttractionSpec now use ordinary short attraction visits (`false`). Training hours/qualifications remain persisted independently; no new runtime patch, normalization or fallback.
- Native pause already blocks the attraction and fails its active ApplyEffectExecutor. - Native acceptance passed: the old policy returns directly to studying with empty hunger/thirst/sleep; the authored policy releases to root need selection after the session. Pausing fails the current native lesson, rejects incoming students, preserves partial training hours, and resuming restores ordinary study visits. Evidence: `outputs/school-visit283-acceptance.log`. No test mutations saved; original `for codex.timber` and disposable school copy retain SHA256 `D5C3A3322BE63CDE174336240607D19BDB2024170294022F538EC9D0CA23D375`. This verifies native decisions/executors, not the reported mass-death timeline. No release bump, commit, push or Workshop upload.
- Final review: school test session closed cleanly. Reloaded the untouched `passage-reconnect-repro-copy.timber` with the school acceptance flag disabled, ran one second and automatically paused; no uncaught exception (`outputs/school-visit283-review.log`). Original player save remains untouched.
- Separate observation: closing the prior build-282 test session with Alt+F4 produced a native UnityPlayer crash on 2026-11-05 at 09:01:50, without a school test active. Preserved `outputs/passage-reconnect282-review.log` and native `Crash_2026-11-05_160150310`; this is not evidence that school pause caused the reported beaver deaths.

## Radioactive-water pumping crash repair (2026-11-04; build 278 installed; recovery replay passed)

- User's build-275 crash: `RadiationSimulation.MixWaterChanges` reported radioactive share 1.004881 in clean water. The installed DLL reproduces catastrophic cancellation when subtracting almost equal radioactive volumes during near-empty pumping. Compute retained volume directly and derive removed volume from it; keep the existing strict share guard.
- Build 276 fixed that arithmetic but its crash-save replay exposed a second defect. Diagnostic build 277 confirmed cell 6412 started with clean/bad/radioactive volumes 3.97814256E-05 / 0.206209153 / 4.14364476E-05. One pure-badwater removal of 0.149856851 amplified the already-present radioactive surplus. The existing kernel-to-soil transfer incorrectly treated the validation tolerance as surplus that could remain in water. Its producer now measures surplus against `1 - bad` and transfers every positive surplus under the existing soil-deposition rule. No new recovery fallback, post-pump normalization, or relaxed error guard.
- Added lazy failure diagnostics containing the starting volumes, final volumes and relevant native change sequence. Regression harness invokes the actual compiled DLL by reflection: 30,025 checks pass, including the exact live-trace case, near-empty removal, conservation and selective badwater removal. Incremental compile passes (three existing warnings).
- Build 278 is installed through the official builder. All 802 data/manifest/DLL hashes match; Windows and macOS bundles pass platform/material/mesh checks. The installed DLL passes all 30,025 arithmetic checks. Full static suite: 78 pass, one item-use report (52 goods, zero issues), one Git-only check unavailable in the non-Git mirror and passing in the real repo. Native recovery replay completed 600 speed-weighted seconds without exceptions, starting from the original crash snapshot. The user then saved "for codex" through the native UI (Day 341.328125 versus original Day 339.489583343). Its separate copy reloaded with zero dropped objects and no radiation exception. The probe reported a SaveAs callback but did not create its named output; that callback is not counted as persistence evidence. The native user-created save is the verified file. Build 276/277 diagnostic runs loaded the copied crash save with zero dropped objects, then correctly failed on the second defect. Original saves remain untouched. Evidence: `outputs/radiation-crash276-recovery.log`, `outputs/radiation-crash277-trace.log`, `outputs/radiation-crash276/`.
- Includes the previously prepared hospital medicine and workplace UI changes below. Their dedicated treatment/continuity and visual acceptance remain pending; no release version bump or Workshop upload.

Raw ore also crushes to Dirt (3 -> 8).

## Game modes from other mods: radioactive tide settings derived from each mode's weather (Claude Code session, 2026-11-04, gamemodes agent; mirror `gamemodes` on HEAD 28c0a2c; compile-checked, harness run against the shipped DLLs; not run in game)

- **Player crash (Workshop 0.8.5):** "Radioactive tide: game mode Chill has no RadioactiveTideGameModeSpec ... Every game mode needs radioactive tide settings". Chill is zxuiji's Chill Mode (Workshop 3642660958: temperate 350, drought and badtide 15 at 50%, from cycle 0). The coordinator reproduced it with `Documents/Timberborn/Mods/ChillModeRepro`.
- **Root cause:** only Easy, Normal and Hard carried a `RadioactiveTideGameModeSpec` (the `Data/NewGameModes` overlays); `RadioactiveTideNewGameSettings.PostLoad` required one on every preset (main menu), and `RadioactiveTideStartingValues` (new game, Moddable Weathers seeding) required one on the new game's mode. Any mod-added mode threw.
- **Fix (rule, not a fallback):** every mode without its own spec gets settings from its weather (`RadioactiveTideGameModes.For`/`Derive`, `RadioactiveTideModeWeather`, `RadioactiveTideTuning.GameModeCurve`; docs/radiation.md "Settings per game"): hazard share of a cycle, linear along the curve through the vanilla modes (end segments continued, in range for every share), longest tide capped at the mode's longest hazardous season, off without hazards. Easy, Normal and Hard are generated by the same rule (exactly 24/15%/2-4, 14/25%/3-6, 9/35%/5-10), so the three overlays are deleted. Chill: on, from cycle 30, 10%, 1-3 days. Customize fills from the derived values; the main menu logs each derived mode once per run; the game's log line names the mode and the derivation; saves store the values as before (`DeepFrontier.RadioactiveTideSettings`, or Moddable Weathers' own).
- **Nothing else assumes three modes:** `WorkforceSettings` reads any mode's `InjuryChance`; `TrackPresetButtons` matches buttons to every spec; no other code or tool names Easy/Normal/Hard.
- **Checks:** compile check main and Moddable Weathers 0 errors; new `tools/compile-check/radioactive-tide-modes` harness (shipped 1.1.2.8 DLLs) 35/35 (vanilla modes, Chill, Chill new game and save round trip, old save, own spec, custom mode, no and one-day hazards, share 0 and 1, a 167,832-mode grid valid, capped, monotonic, invalid modes throw); `validate_radioactive_tide_content.mjs` passes (curve against vanilla blueprints, ranges at share 0 and 1, overlays gone, harness table equals the curve). Full `validate_all` not run (user: ship the fix, they play-test).
- **In game, to check:** with the ChillModeRepro mod enabled, New game: the mode screen opens and lists Chill; select Chill, Customize: "Radioactive tides" on, 29 cycles without, 10%, 1 to 3 days; Hard still 9 / 35% / 5 to 10. `Player.log`: "Radioactive tide settings for game mode ... derived from its weather settings" once per mode. Start a Chill game: "Radioactive tide settings: on, from cycle 30, 10% per cycle, 1-3 days (the new game's mode GameMode.Chill, derived from its weather: ...)"; save, reload: "(this save)". Disable the repro mod and load that save: it loads with the same values.

## Moving parts block connection faces in every pose; float32 depth margin (Claude Code session, 2026-11-03, minefaces agent; mirror `minefaces` on HEAD 7595216 + this change, others' uncommitted work pinned to HEAD; generated and validated; not built or run in game)

- **User (via the coordinator, approving the two follow-ups of the eave fix):** tighten the moving-part check so no connection face sits in front of a moving part, accepting that shafts on the dropped spots disconnect; then add the 0.05 rounding fix so the recessed-panel walls connect, the Greenhouse fan wall staying excluded.
- **Moving-part test (`measure_connection_faces.py` `moving_in_front`, `_poses`, `triangles_in_box`):** every pose of every animated part (each baked frame of each clip, plus the rest pose; identical poses deduplicated) is tested triangle by triangle (separating-axis test) against the face's outward cell, extended `FRONT` 0.02 behind the face and `ANIMATED_INSET` 1e-4 in from its sides (touching the boundary does not count). Before: the rest pose on the 8 x 8 face rays. Timbermesh node animations are baked per frame (plugin `animation_builder.py`); the exports carry one "Default" clip of 61-481 frames and no vertex animations (the test refuses vertex animations and chains with differing clips). Roofs use the same test: `roof.animated` and the roof-deck rule "no animated part above" follow it.
  - **A roof split was tried and dropped:** keeping roofs platformable when a moving part only reaches within 0.31 of the cell above's edges (the static roof rule) would have made the Deep Mine's head deck under the sheave wheels platformable (each wheel stands just inside 0.31 of a cell edge).
- **Float32 margin (`PRECISION` 1e-5):** the plane band and the wall-depth and front limits compare depths with 1e-5 instead of 1e-9; exported positions are float32 and a 0.05 inset reads 0.050000191 (under 64 units float32 rounds by up to 3.8e-6).
- **Measured flat faces 4,163 -> 4,125** (52 dropped by the moving-part test, 14 added by the margin, 0 others). Moving parts in the dropped faces' cells reach 0.0001-0.45 in from the cell sides; 9 were connection faces.
- **Connection changes (power transputs / pipe ports, HEAD -> now):**
  - Fertilizer Works 32 -> 30 / 14 -> 12: (2,1,1) Right and (3,2,1) Down, the open layer-2 bay where `#DrierDrum` turns (0.05 into the cell, also at rest).
  - Fishery Works 55 -> 54 / 18 -> 17: (5,0,0) Down, front right tile, `#FisheryWorks.CarvedSign` (0.02).
  - Technical School 33 -> 31: (2,1,1) Down and (3,0,1) Left, the open layer-2 front bay of `#Flywheel` and `#Governor` (0.02).
  - Launch Pad 88 -> 86: roof (2,1,0) and (2,2,0) Top, `#LaunchPad.PumpFlywheel` rim 0.02-0.05 into the cells above.
  - Radioactive Waste Refinery 59 -> 57: roof (2,0,1) and (2,1,1) Top, `#SpentFuelCask` passes 0.31+ through the cells above, only while moving (the rest pose is clear).
  - Deep Mine 76 -> 80 / 44 -> 48: back wall (1,4,0) and (2,4,0) Up (brick panels 0.050000191 behind the pilasters), plus (0,3,0) and (1,3,0) Down, the bath house's front wall facing the ore bin inside the mine's own ground cells. Those two are inert: the Deep Ore Vein fills those cells (Floor, Bottom, Corners, Path, Middle), so no shaft or valve can stand there, like the mine's existing ground-layer interior transputs.
  - Steel Mill 80 -> 83: (1,3,3), (2,3,3), (3,3,3) Down, the layer-4 wall over the roof.
  - Deep Well 4 -> 5: (0,0,1) Right. Water Treatment Plant 81 -> 82 / 31 -> 32: (7,4,0) Up.
  - Greenhouse unchanged: (5,1,0) Right gains the margin but `#GableFanR`'s blades sweep the top of its outward cell (0.89-0.98 of the layer), so it stays excluded.
  - New faces' outward cells hold no moving part in any pose (by the test) and no static geometry beyond trim, except the two inert Deep Mine faces (ore bin).
- **Roof decks (`tools/roof_decks.json`, regenerated):** platformable cells lost: Launch Pad (2,1,0), (2,2,0) and Radioactive Waste Refinery (2,0,1), (2,1,1) (now excluded, "animated part above"; their blocks lose Stackable); Observation Deck (2,0,4) moves from platformable to excluded in the list but its terrace block stays Stackable (authored by its generator), so nothing changes there in game. Roof hardpoints unchanged.
- **Saves:** a shaft or valve on a dropped connection face disconnects; anything standing on the four Launch Pad and Refinery roof cells loses its support and may be removed at load. CHANGELOG says so.
- **Validator (`validate_connection_exports.py`):** `MOVING_CASES` (Fertilizer Works drier drum, Fishery Works sign, Greenhouse gable fan, Refinery cask (moving only), Launch Pad flywheel rim: each one plane with a moving part in front, and not a flat face) and `RELIEF_CASES` (the Deep Mine's back walls: relief in (0.05, 0.05 + PRECISION], flat). Mutations with the records regenerated under each mutant: rest pose only fails the Refinery case; parts having to reach 0.05 into the cell fails the Fishery and Launch Pad cases; a 1e-9 margin fails the Deep Mine relief cases (and the Greenhouse case, whose wall is then not one plane).
- **Speed:** measuring all 84 models takes about 28 s (18 s before; the first exact version 76 s); `exports-connection` 30.7 s (11 s before): poses deduplicated, each part rejected by its bounds over all poses before its per-pose bounds, triangles by their own bounds before the separating-axis test. ## Walls under a roof's eave take shafts and pipes: the Deep Mine's lamp house (Claude Code session, 2026-11-03, minefaces agent; mirror `minefaces` on HEAD b609186 + this change, others' uncommitted work pinned to HEAD; generated and validated; not built or run in game)

- **User (0.8.5, in game):** "Deep mine needs some help. I tried attaching power to the side of it on the second or third level (over a window) and it didn't take the connection where it obviously should have."
- **The face:** the brick lamp house's outer side wall (the side away from the headframe), layer 2, tiles (0,0,1) and (0,1,1) Left, with its two arched windows. Layer 3 on that side is the plank gable roof itself (54-degree slope), not a wall.
- **Why it failed (measured on the export, 8x8 rays):** not the windows. The glass sits 0.002 proud of the cell plane and the brick 0.05 behind it, so one plane covers 77-78% of the face (60% needed). The roof's eave is at 1.84, so its slope (normal 53.9 degrees off the axis) covers the top sample row, and `measure_connection_faces.py` rejected any covering sample more than 5 degrees off the axis ("no curve or slope, even partly"). The record listed both faces under `curvedFaces` as `slope`.
- **Deep Mine faces at HEAD, by layer** (outer walls and faces onto open cells; table from the record, the blueprint and the face test):
  - Layer 1: left wall all 5 tiles connect; front x 0, 2, 3, 4 (x 1 is the door, the Entrance in front); right wall all 5; back x 0, 3, 4. Back x 1 and x 2 fail the one-plane test: brick panels 0.05 behind the pilasters, stored as 0.050000191, split each wall into two planes of 53% and 58% (follow-up below). Bottom on all 25 cells; Top on the platformable decks (3,0), (0,3), (1,3), (1,4).
  - Layer 2: lamp-house gable front (0,0) and (1,0) Down connect; the ore bin's boarded side (0,2) Left and back (0,2), (1,2) Up connect; headframe right plating (4,1) Right connects. The plating sits 0.3 inside the tile line, so the leg tiles (4,0) and (4,2) are 33% covered or sloped (column bevels); the open front shows the skips (animated, (3,1) Down); the chimney (0,4) is round; the winding-engine deck at the back (2..4, 3..4) is open machinery (3-42% covered, backstay slopes 48 degrees).
  - Layer 3: the lamp-house roof slope (0,0), (0,1) Left and its gable triangle (25-28% covered); the ore bin's top 38%; headframe right plating (4,1) Right connects, (4,0) and (4,2) 28-33%; chimney and backstays.
  - Layer 4: head deck Top (2,0), (3,0), (4,0) and plating (4,1) Right connect; (2,1) Left is the ore chute's sloped top (46 degrees); the rest open or chimney.
  - Layers 5-6: sheaves and their posts, animated or 0-6% covered.
- **Native evidence** (installed 1.1.2.8 models read from resources.assets with UnityPy, measured with the same test; nothing written to the repository): vanilla Iron Teeth consumers author side transputs on walls whose only non-flat covering is the roof's eave in the top band: Gear Workshop (60.1 degrees), Industrial Lumber Mill (69.3), Fermenter (23.2) and Food Factory (45.1) in the top eighth, Motivatorium (30.0) in the top quarter. Vanilla shaft geometry: `AxleHorizontal.IronTeeth` spans 0.376-0.624 of the layer and `ShaftFrame.IronTeeth` 0.031-0.68, so a wall flat up to 0.75 takes everything a shaft puts against it. Vanilla walls also carry transputs over plinth slopes at their foot (Smelter, Coffee Brewery, Explosives Factory); the rule does not adopt that (the shaft frame starts at 0.031).
- **Fix (the rule, every building):** `measure_connection_faces.py` `eave_samples`, `EAVE_BAND` 0.25. On a side face, covering samples in the top quarter whose normal turns from the face axis up toward the sky (Z > 0, within 5 degrees of the vertical plane through the axis) are the roof's edge: they are left out of the axis and spread angles, the curve test and the one-plane share. The flat wall below still needs 60% of the face. Top and Bottom faces are unchanged. Records gain `eaveFaces` (and `stats.eaveFaces`); the rule text names the band.
- **Result (measured flat faces 4,135 -> 4,163, +28, none lost; 14 new connection faces on 6 buildings, power transputs +12, pipe ports +13):**
  - Deep Mine: power 74 -> 76, pipes 42 -> 44: (0,0,1) and (0,1,1) Left.
  - Mission Control: 62 -> 66, 32 -> 36: layer 3, right wall (4,2,2), (4,3,2), (4,4,2) Right and back (2,4,2) Up.
  - Precision Machine Works: 52 -> 55, 28 -> 31: layer 3, back wall (0,3,2), (1,3,2), (2,3,2) Up.
  - Radioactive Waste Refinery: 57 -> 59, 25 -> 27: layer 2, right wall (4,2,1), (4,3,1) Right.
  - Archive Excavation: power 27 -> 28: ground layer, back (1,3,0) Up.
  - Radioactive Water Outfall: pipes 12 -> 14: layer 2, back (0,1,1), (1,1,1) Up.
  - Food Packaging Works and Radioactive Waste Vault gain measured faces only (neighbour not exterior, or no power/liquids there). Nothing else in Data changes (canonical generation against HEAD: the six blueprints, the 84 records' rule text and `eaveFaces`, and the four tool and doc files).
- **Nothing sticks out or moves in front of a new face** (32 x 32 probe of the neighbour cell, finer than the measurement's 8 x 8): no animated geometry on any of the 14; static geometry beyond the face plane only trim, the same relief as already-accepted faces on the same walls: the lamp house's corner posts 0.04 at the tile edge and its window frames 0.027, Precision Machine Works 0.064 window frames, the refinery 0.045-0.05 panels, Archive Excavation a 0.05 post at one edge, Mission Control 0.05-0.095 bands and window panels (identical on its accepted layer-2 faces), the outfall 0.02.

Mutations, each with the records regenerated under the mutant so only these cases can catch it: the old rule (band 0) fails the Deep Mine eave case; band 0.375 the Oil Power Plant case; any tilt the ore-chute case; any turn the hangar case; the foot of the wall exempt too the Sandstone Pit case.
  - **Generation.** Canonical generation in the mirror reaches a fixed point (a second measure + generation run leaves Data and records byte-identical).

Full `validate_all` (with the Blender source checks) in the mirror: 77 of 79 pass; `no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only (0 of 51 goods need work).
- **Follow-ups found, not changed here:**
  - **Plane band at float32 precision.** Relief exactly 0.05 deep is stored as 0.050000191 and misses the "within 0.05" band (1e-9 margin), splitting a flat wall into two planes. A 1e-5 margin would add 15 measured faces: Deep Mine back (1,4,0), (2,4,0) Up and (0,3,0), (1,3,0) Down, Steel Mill (1..3,3,3) Down, Deep Well (0,0,1) Right, Water Treatment Plant (7,4,0) Up, Greenhouse (5,1,0) Right, Launch Pad 2, Reinforced Dam Block 2, Archive Excavation 1.
