## Deep Drill bores Deep Ore Sources; empty sources recharge slowly (Claude Code session, 2026-11-03, orerefill agent; mirror `orerefill` on HEAD 70c37c4 + this change, others' uncommitted work pinned to HEAD; compile-checked; generated and validated; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "make the deep drill something which replenishes every so often, in case the user doesn't make it in time to make the permanent solution. It should be painful though. And show as a cooldown or something." Then: "the recipe says it needs deep ore and water to produce deep ore? That makes no sense." Coordinator (user decision): the drill draws from any Deep Ore Source in its district, not a range.
- **Design (docs/progression.md "Deep Ore Source recharge", docs/source-authoring.md):**
  - **Recipe.** `Recipe.DeepOre` is 3 Water -> 1 Deep Ore. Same id, so saved drills keep their recipe and progress.
  - **`DeepDrill` (new, `Scripts/DeepOre/DeepDrill.cs`, `DeepDrillSpec {GoodId}`).** A native `IManufactoryLimiter`:
    - it takes the cycle's unit from a deposit in `MaxProductionProgressChange` and keeps it (saved `CycleOreDrawn`) until `ProductionFinished`;
    - `ProductionEfficiency` is 0 while it holds none and no deposit of its district holds enough;
    - the deposit is the nearest by `Accessible.FindRoadPath`, as `DistrictInventoryPicker.ClosestInventoryWithStock` picks, then the one holding most.
  - **`DeepOreDeposit` (new, `DeepOreDepositSpec {GoodId, Capacity 10000, RechargeAmount 20, RechargeTimeInDays 5}`).** It replaces the source's `StockpileSpec`/`FixedStockpileSpec`; entrance and `BuildingAccessibleSpec` kept for the district. The amount is saved. Once empty, a native `ITimeTrigger` recharges 20 after 5 days and stops until the source is drilled empty again (the bush rule). Its progress is saved; the timer never runs in the map editor. New sources hold Capacity, as the native map editor fills a new fixed stockpile.
  - **Panels (the game's own views).** Source: `RuinFragment` (ore left), `ResourceYieldFragment` (calendar-cycle icon, "5 days", progress, 20 Deep Ore; `resource-yield--inactive` while stocked), "Next 20 deep ore in N days." while recharging. Drill: the district's sources' ore, the soonest recharge once all are empty, or "No Deep Ore Source in the district.". Map editor: the native `FixedStockpileFragment` amount field (good dropdown hidden) with native undo.
  - **Statuses.** Drill: "Deep Ore Sources recharging" (Empty, floating icon) and "No Deep Ore Source in the district" (NothingToDo, alert). No notices.
- **Why not native gatherables (decompiled 1.1.2.8):** `GatherableYieldGrower` needs `Growable`/`LivingNaturalResource`/`DyingNaturalResource`; a gather empties the whole yield and regrowth restores the spec yield; `Yielder` amounts come only from the spec and are removed by flag workers, never by a manufactory.
- **Numbers.**
  - One Deep Ore Processor eats 42.7 Deep Ore a day on Deep Iron (2 workers, 16 h).
  - One empty source gives 4 a day (9%); maps give 8 or 12.
  - Deep Mine 300 Deep Ore, Steel Mill 270 (`ProgramModel`): about 38 days of recharge for the mine on a two-source map (25 on three), 71 (48) with the Steel Mill.
  - Every building up to the mine, 1,437 Deep Ore: 180 or 120 days, against about 17 days of research from the drill to the mine.
- **Map checks (`prepare_deep_frontier_map.py`, `map_loop_requirements.json`).**
  - The finite budget ignores the recharge.
  - New hard check `deepOreRechargeMinDays` 60: all drillable sources recharging must need at least 60 days for that budget's need. Every built-in map: 120-180 days (`loopCoverage.program.deepOreRecharge`).
  - Placement checks read `DeepOreDepositSpec` and `DeepDrillSpec` (`deep_drill_bored`), keep the entrance check, and `build` writes `DeepFrontier.DeepOreDeposit {Amount}`.
  - `map_setup_content.mjs` and `BuiltinMapSetup` set the amount through `DeepOreDeposit.SetAmount`.
  - `MapLoopAcceptance` reads the deposit.
- **Saves (statically reasoned, not loaded).**
  - Native `EntitiesLoader` ignores saved components the template lost (`FixedStockpile`, `SingleGoodAllower`, other mods' stockpile parts).
  - `DeepOreDeposit.Load` reads an old source's `Inventory:Stockpile` Deep Ore as its amount (native `GoodRegistry` serializer; another good is logged and dropped). This also covers the four map files built before.
  - An emptied old source (nothing saved) starts recharging; empty sources never disappeared.
  - A drill saved mid-cycle (progress above 0) keeps that cycle's ore.
  - Old Deep Ore in a drill's inventory is ordinary output.
  - The user's current save (read-only copy of `furrealsmira` day 17-16, Waterfalls) has both sources at 8,500, a drill 43 tiles from the nearest, and a built Deep Mine. If the sources share the drill's district it starts drilling, otherwise it shows the no-source status.
- **Deadlock audit (coordinator, after 70c37c4).** `tools/validate_deadlocks.mjs`:
  - `DeepOre` is now a slow source (`MAP_SOURCE_KIND` `slow`), renewable only through the recharge. It must match the map-editor deposits that recharge (`DeepOreDepositSpec` with a positive recharge), and it cannot be both finite and slow.
  - A map source building's recipe that makes its own map good (the Deep Drill's) counts only as the map source, never as a free recipe.
  - New `slow-replacement`: a producer besides the slow source (the Deep Mine) must be buildable and runnable at its unlock from the recharge alone.
  - The four accepted entries (D3 `rebuild:DeepMine`, `finite-replacement:DeepOre`; D4 both quarries) failed as stale and are removed.
  - `docs/deadlock-audit.md`: D3 and D4 resolved, D7 done, model section updated. The off-path deep ore (steel structures, coal plant, metallurgy, the quarries' 35 Deep Iron) and how the recharge covers it are in docs/progression.md.
- **Costs.** None change. `material_costs --write` changes only the recorded `DeepOre` recipe; the material rule still values Deep Ore at 1 in every world (3 Water at 0.33 plus time is 1.03), and `lategame_costs --check` passes.
- **Files:**
  - `Scripts/DeepOre/DeepOreDeposit.cs`, `DeepDrill.cs`, `DeepOrePanels.cs` (new);
  - `Scripts/MapSetup/BuiltinMapSetup.cs`, `MapSetupSpecs.cs`, `MapLoopAcceptance.cs`, `DeepFrontierRuntime.cs`;
  - `tools/create_vertical_slice_content.mjs`, `validate_vertical_slice_content.mjs`, `prepare_deep_frontier_map.py`, `test_prepare_deep_frontier_map.py`, `map_loop_requirements.json`, `map_setup_content.mjs`, `automation_content.json` (`DeepDrillSpec` Off response), `progression_data.mjs`, `validate_deadlocks.mjs`, `material_cost_context.json`;
  - generated `DeepDrill`, `DeepOreSource`, `Recipe.DeepOre`, `enUS.csv`;
  - docs: progression, maps, source-authoring, implementation-plan, deadlock-audit;
  - Timberborn skill `verified-findings.md`.
- **Checks:**
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check` in the mirror: 0 errors.
  - **Generation.** Canonical generation differs from HEAD only in the 4 generated files above, at a material-cost fixed point (`material_costs --check`).
  - **Validators.** `validate_vertical_slice_content.mjs` checks: the water-only recipe; the deposit instead of a stockpile, with entrance and district access; recharge at most 1% of capacity; one source's daily recharge at most a tenth of one processor's hungriest daily Deep Ore.
  - **Maps.** `prepare_deep_frontier_map.py builtin`: all 19 maps OK. `test_prepare_deep_frontier_map.py`: 24/24, with 2 new cases (wrong deposit good; recharge more than a trickle).
  - **validate_all.** `-Only` automation_content, building_descriptions, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, map_setup, priority_statuses, single_inventory, inventory_enable_guard, load_order, preview_components, vertical_slice_content, workforce_risks, bot_upkeep, audit-progression, good_migrations, deep_frontier_manifest, status_icon_anchors: 17/17. `deadlocks` passes (DeepOre slow, Deep Mine reachable from the recharge, 0 accepted). Full `validate_all -SkipBlender` in the mirror on 70c37c4 + this change: 71 of 73 pass. `audit-item-uses` is report-only (0 of 51 goods need work). `no-game-assets` needs git, so it fails in the mirror; `check_no_game_assets.py` passes on this commit's tree.
- **In game, to check (not run; the user is playing).** Use a copy of a save in a test settlement, never Memory Test autosaves the user plays in:
  1. Select a Deep Ore Source:
     - the panel shows its ore (native ruin row) and a greyed regrow row (calendar-cycle icon, "5 days", 20 deep ore);
     - there is no stockpile inventory, and haulers never take from it.
  2. Select (or build) a Deep Drill on the same path network, with water, power and Metal Block bits:
     - its recipe shows 3 water -> 1 deep ore;
     - the source's number drops by one per cycle;
     - its panel shows the district's ore ("Deep ore in district sources (N)").
  3. In the Map Editor, place a Deep Ore Source next to flat ground and set its amount field to 3 (Ctrl+Z restores the old value). Save, start a game on that map, connect the source and a Deep Drill by path, and let the drill empty it. Expected:
     - the source shows "Next 20 deep ore in 5.0 days" with the progress rising;
     - the drill shows the same recharge row and the status "Deep Ore Sources recharging" (Empty icon);
     - at the fastest speed, after 5 days the source holds 20, the drill bores them out and waits again.
  4. Save while the source recharges, reload: the progress and countdown continue where they were.
  5. Delete the path to the source: the drill's status reads "No Deep Ore Source in the district" (alert).
  6. Load a save from before this change (a copy): sources keep their ore, an emptied source starts its 5-day recharge, and drills work. `Player.log` shows no `[DeepFrontier]` errors.

## Deadlock audit: Deep Mine drill bits on Metal Block, tools/validate_deadlocks.mjs (Claude Code session, 2026-11-03, deadlock agent; mirror `deadlock` on HEAD 279e9ae + this change; generated and validated; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "Let's find anywhere the user can deadlock themselves and change the recipes up a bit. Please look through." Coordinator: cover the Technical School programs against the specialist-only buildings (the beaver path must work without the Reactor Bot bypass); leave the Deep Drill and Deep Ore Source to the Deep Drill agent.
- **Findings:** `docs/deadlock-audit.md` (D1-D7, soft risks S1-S13, proposals P1-P3).
- **Fixed (D1):** the Deep Mine wore 0.1 Steel per full-crew hour as drill bits, and production stops without them (`WorkplaceUpkeep.cs`). Steel is made from mined deep ore, so once the finite Deep Ore Sources and the steel stock were spent the mine could never restart, and Metallurgy (trained on Deep Iron) could not be retrained for the Steel Mill. Before the fix, 25 goods failed the new renewable-economy check, every one behind "DeepOre.Mine@DeepMine (needs HighGradeSteel to run)".
  - `tools/workforce_risks.json` `workplaceUpkeep`: Deep Mine `[["MetalBlock", 0.3, "DrillBits"], ["Concrete", 0.1, "ShaftLining"]]` (was HighGradeSteel 0.1, role `SteelDrillBits`, now removed). 0.3 = 0.1 Steel by raw effort (11.45 / 4.2), rounded up.
  - Generated changes against HEAD: `DeepMine.IronTeeth.blueprint.json` (the upkeep good, rate and role), `enUS.csv` (Deep Mine description "Uses up metal blocks as drill bits and concrete as shaft lining; stops without them."; the three `DeepFrontier.WorkplaceUpkeep.SteelDrillBits.*` rows removed). No construction cost, recipe or science changed (`material_costs --check` matches, `lategame_costs --check` exit 0). `docs/item-use-audit.md` regenerated: Steel loses "Upkeep: DeepMine"; the file also picks up the Hospital cost change of 8bea9ab.
  - Old saves: a saved Steel part unit at a mine is dropped with a log warning (`WorkplaceUpkeep.Load`), and Steel stocked at the mine becomes a leftover that haulers carry out. Not tested in game.
- **Verified (D2):** Metallurgy (Steel Mill): Metal Block, Deep Iron, Corn Ration, none needing metallurgists after D1. Nuclear (reactor): Electronics, Yellowcake, Medicine. Flight (Launch Pad): Electronics, Recovered Telemetry from Mission Control's Guidance telemetry (beaver crew, 75,000, before the 200,000 pad), Canned Fish.
- **Accepted by design, with a stale check:** rebuilding the last Deep Mine and the first mine both need deep ore (D3); the quarries' Deep Iron against the Deep Mine's concrete lining (D4).
- **Proposals (not made):** P1 an Outfall recipe for Contaminated Water, which tides make from cycle 15 while nothing takes it before 300,000 and its storage cannot be deleted (D5); P2 the Reactor Charging Bay 400,000 -> 300,000, so Reactor Bots never exist without a place to charge (D6); P3 Deep Mine shaft lining Concrete -> Brick (D4). Deep-ore items for the coordinator: D7.
- **New check `tools/validate_deadlocks.mjs`** (validate_all `deadlocks`): workplace upkeep, goods-consuming buildings, the Deep Well's bits, specialist disciplines (school programs) and food services gate each building's recipes. Checks: `renewable-economy`, `discipline-trainable`, `operating-unlock` (Deep Frontier buildings), `rebuild`, `finite-replacement`, `map-source-kind` (every MAP_SOURCES good classified finite or renewable), `accepted-stale`. On HEAD's data it fails (29); on the new data it passes with the four accepted entries. Fault injection in the mirror fails for: Deep Mine on Steel bits, Metallurgy needing Steel, Mission Control without Guidance telemetry, Deep Drill on Steel bits, Coal Power Plant at 1,000 science, Deep Ore Processor costing Deep Iron, a grocery with 2 foods available.
- **Checks (mirror, others' uncommitted files pinned to HEAD):** canonical generation changes only the two files above; `validate_all -Only` deadlocks, bot_upkeep, workforce_risks, building_descriptions, vertical_slice_content, audit-progression (0 failures, 4 warnings as before), automation_content, super_bots, reactor_bot_workplaces, deep_well_content pass; full `validate_all -SkipBlender` (mirror on 8bea9ab + this change) 71 of 73 pass (`audit-item-uses` report-only; `no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index). Rebased on 279e9ae (Technical School supplies): regenerated, the same two generated files differ, and the targeted checks above pass again.

## Technical School supplies follow the taught profession (Claude Code session, 2026-11-03, school agent; mirror `school` on HEAD 8bea9ab + this change, others' uncommitted work pinned to HEAD; compile-checked; generated and validated; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "The requirements for keeping the building stocked should change depending on what is being taught." The player saw every program's supplies, which read as if metallurgy needed Electronics.
- **What the player saw (decompiled 1.1.2.8):**
  - **Supply panel.** Native `GoodConsumingBuildingFragment` builds its `InventoryFragment` with `ShowEmptyRows`, one row per allowed good of the inventory, which allows all 8 goods of the template's `GoodConsumingBuildingSpec`. The 5 refused goods showed as "0 / 0"; the taught goods' limits were the shared highest rate (a reactor school stocked 20 Electronics, not 16). With nothing taught the hours-left label read `float.MaxValue`.
  - **Tooltip.** Native `GoodConsumingBuildingDescriber` lists the consumer's goods for preview and unfinished objects. `PreviewFactory` never runs `InitializeEntity`, so the tool tooltip listed all 8 goods at their rates. The building description itself names no supplies.
  - **Hours-left bar.** `MaximumWorkingTime` was computed in native `Awake` from all 8 goods.
  - **Haulers, status, attraction: already right.** `SpecialistSchool` gives the native consumer a spec copy with the taught goods (`CanUse` → `Attraction.IsUsable`, `HoursUntilNoSupply`, the lack-of-supplies status read it) and, as the inventory's `IGoodDisallower` (`SchoolSupplyInventory`), returned 0 for other goods, so `Inventory.GetCapacity` never requested them. The class comment's "another discipline's stock stays inside" was wrong: leftovers are native unwanted stock that `RemoveUnwantedStockLaborBehavior` carries out.
- **Change:**
  - **Native capacity per program.** `SpecialistSchool.AllowedAmount` returns the taught good's native capacity, ceil(goods per hour x `FullInventoryWorkHours` 40), the same formula as `GoodConsumingBuildingInventoryInitializer`: Metallurgy 60 Metal Block, 30 Deep Iron, 30 Corn Ration; Nuclear 16 Electronics, 8 Yellowcake, 16 Medicine; Flight 20 Electronics, 10 Recovered Telemetry, 20 Canned Fish. Other goods 0.
  - `ApplySupplies` also sets `MaximumWorkingTime` by the native formula over the taught goods (private setter, resolved at load).
  - **Panel and tooltip (new `SchoolSupplyPanel.cs`, Harmony, schools only, `Verify()` at mod start).** A postfix on `GoodConsumingBuildingFragment.UpdateFragment` hides rows by the native workshop rule (`InventoryRowUpdater.ShouldShow`: allowed amount > 0 or in stock), so the taught goods and leftovers being carried out show, and hides the whole supply fragment while nothing is taught. A postfix on `GoodConsumingBuildingDescriber.DescribeEntity` drops the input section while nothing is taught (the preview always). An unfinished school with a profession chosen shows that profession's supplies natively.
  - **School panel.** New line "Supplies used per hour while students are inside: …" from `SpecialistSchool.Supplies`; the program line drops its supplies clause.
  - Switching profession fires `DisallowedGoodsChanged` for every good, so requests, limits and unwanted stock update on the click.
- **Why Harmony:** both native views read the inventory or spec directly with no per-entity filter. Per-program allowed goods on the one inventory would invalidate in-flight capacity reservations (`UnreserveCapacity` throws for a good no longer allowed). Mutating them would also leave the district registries stale.
- **Saves:** no new data. Stock and the saved supply list load as before; a saved school's other-program stock becomes unwanted stock on load and is carried out.
- **Files:** `Scripts/Workforce/SpecialistSchool.cs`, `SchoolSupplyPanel.cs` (new), `WorkforcePanels.cs`; `tools/workforce_risk_content.mjs` (1 new row, 1 shortened); `tools/validate_workforce_risks.mjs`; generated `enUS.csv`; Timberborn skill `dependency-apis.md` ("Goods-consuming buildings whose supplies change at runtime").
- **Checks:**
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check` in the mirror: 0 errors (3 existing warnings).
  - **Validator.** `validate_workforce_risks.mjs` now checks the runtime sources (taught-only `Supplies`, native capacity in `AllowedAmount`, `MaximumWorkingTime`, the disallower, both patches applied after `Verify()` and their rules, the panel listing only `Supplies`). It also checks the native contract decompiled from the plugin assemblies, matched without access modifiers since they are publicized. Mutations (`int.MaxValue` capacity, the row rule without stock, the panel listing all disciplines) fail as they should.
  - **Generation.** Canonical generation in the mirror changes only 2 `enUS.csv` rows against HEAD.
  - **validate_all.** `-Only` workforce_risks, single_inventory, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, building_descriptions, vertical_slice_content, automation_content, inventory_enable_guard, load_order, mod_dependencies: 10/10. Full `validate_all -SkipBlender`: 70 of 73 pass in the mirror (`deadlocks` is another agent's uncommitted validator, not in HEAD, failing on finite-stock goods unrelated to this change; `no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only).
  - **Skill.** `quick_validate.py` not run (no PyYAML on this machine; only a reference file changed).
- **In game, to check (not run).** Use a test save with a finished, powered Technical School and stock of all eight goods in the district:
  1. Select a school that teaches nothing. Expected: "Choose the profession…", no supply panel at the bottom, no huge hours-left number.
  2. Hover the Technical School in the Science toolbar. Expected: the tooltip shows cost, power and description, with no supplies section.
  3. Click "Teach Metallurgists". Expected: the supply panel shows only the rows Metal block 0 / 60, Deep iron 0 / 30 and Corn ration 0 / 30, and the school panel reads "Supplies used per hour while students are inside: 1.5 Metal blocks, 0.75 Deep iron, 0.75 Corn rations". Haulers bring only those three.
  4. After some stock arrives, click "Teach Reactor operators". Expected: at once the panel shows Electronics 0 / 16, Yellowcake 0 / 8 and Medicine 0 / 16, plus the metallurgy goods as "N / 0" while haulers carry them out. Those rows disappear when empty, and only the reactor goods are delivered.
  5. Switch to Flight. Expected: Electronics' limit becomes 20 and it stays; Yellowcake and Medicine are carried out.
  6. With students inside, let a taught good run out. Expected: students stop coming and the lack-of-supplies status names only the taught goods. The hours-left bar is full at a full stock.
  7. Save and reload. Expected: the profession, stock, limits and rows are the same.
  8. Place a new school and pick a profession while it is under construction. Expected: its tooltip lists only that profession's supplies.

## Hospital without Alloy and Machine Core: per-building family goods (Claude Code session, 2026-11-03, hospcost agent; mirror `hospcost` on HEAD 2c73b75 + this change; generated and validated; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "Can we remove machine cores and alloy from the requirements to build the hospital?"
- **Before:** the Hospital (50,000 science, T4 frontier) priced its metal family in the T4 goods (`familyGoods.T4.metal` = 4 Alloy : 1 Machine Core): 475 Treated Plank, 20 Alloy, 5 Machine Core, 440 Concrete, 460 Brick, 180 Gear. It cures irradiated beavers and must stay reachable before radioactive tides (cycle 15).
- **Rule:** new `materialRule.familyGoodsOverrides` in `tools/building_construction_costs.json`, `{"Hospital": {"families": {"metal": "T3"}, "reason": ...}}`. `material_costs.mjs` `familyGoodsFor(id, tier)` returns the tier's family goods with the overridden families taken from the named stage; `materialComposition` and `mergedFamilies(id, tier)` use it. The family's value share, the total basis value, rounding, science and tier are unchanged.
- **After:** 475 Treated Plank, 35 Metal Block, 20 Deep Iron, 20 Steel, 440 Concrete, 460 Brick, 180 Gear. Canonical generation in the mirror (others' uncommitted tool-group files pinned to HEAD) changes only `Hospital.IronTeeth.blueprint.json` against HEAD; `material_costs --check` matches; `lategame_costs --write` leaves the catalog byte-identical (the Hospital's cost is authored in `frontier_progression.json` and goes through `buildingCost`).
- **Validation (fails at load, no default):** `familyGoodsOverrides` must exist (an object); each entry has exactly `families` and `reason`, a non-empty reason, known families and tiers, a measured building; the named tier must be earlier than the building's own; every good it names must be producible without the building at its science. `audit_progression.mjs` checks the overridden family's goods and value share in the named stage's goods and fails `material-override-unknown` for an override on a building the rule does not price.
- **Checks:** audit on HEAD's data (old cost) fails `material-rule-goods` for the Hospital; mutations fail as they should (T5 and T4 overrides: not an earlier stage; empty reason; unknown family; unknown building; key removed). `validate_all -Only` audit-progression, construction_materials, vertical_slice_content, power_rule, radiation_content, workforce_risks, single_inventory, component_lifecycle, building_descriptions: 9/9. Full `validate_all -SkipBlender` in the mirror: 70 of 72 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only).
- **In game, to check (not run):** unlock the Hospital (50,000 science): its tooltip and construction site ask for 475 Treated Plank, 35 Metal Block, 20 Deep Iron, 20 Steel, 440 Concrete, 460 Brick and 180 Gear, and no Alloy or Machine Core.

## Reactor meltdown: crater, 6-tile blast, crater flood and circular radii (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, meltdown agent; mirror `meltdown` on HEAD 42316a2 + this change; compile-checked; generated and validated; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "Reactor meltdown should destroy the terrain underneath it as well. The radiation of the blocks also needs to not just be square. It should be circular." Then: "it should also destroy buildings and structures within 6 tiles of the reactor" and "the radioactive water should be everywhere enough to spread past the crater we make".
- **Before:** at heat 1, `MeltdownService` deleted only the reactor (`EntityService.Delete`), terrain untouched. Every area was a Chebyshev square around the footprint: kill 2 tiles, irradiation dose 16, soil 0.5 → 0.16 and dissolved water 2.0 → 0.2 over 12; 60 radioactive units over the footprint plus a saved 0.5/h source for 240 h. Waste leaks irradiated a 3-tile square.
- **Circle (`BuildingSite`):** straight-line tiles from the nearest footprint tile, a column within R when the distance rounds to R or less (dx² + dy² ≤ R² + R). Now used by the 12-tile contamination area and its falloff, the 2-tile kill and 16-tile irradiation radii, the new blast radius and the vault/refinery leak soil (3 tiles). Measured from the walls (edge, not centre): around a 1x1 footprint an exact tile circle, around the 14x11 reactor the footprint swept by a circle (12 tiles: 1,194 columns instead of 1,330).
- **Crater:** elliptical bowl centred on the footprint, semi-axes half-extents + `MeltdownCraterRim` 2 (9 x 7.5), only columns within 2 tiles, depth max(1, ceil(`MeltdownCraterDepth` 4 x (1 - u))); cut below the ground the reactor stood on, never z 0. Flat ground: 214 columns (every footprint tile), 530 blocks. Rim ≤ kill radius, so everyone on it dies.
- **Blast:** every `BuildingSpec` object (where native `DeconstructionSystemConfigurator` decorates `Deconstructible`: everything placed with the construction tools, finished or not) with a block in a column within `MeltdownBlastRadius` 6, any height. Natural resources, ruins and map objects stay unless on removed ground. Goods in destroyed buildings drop as native recovered-goods stacks (`DeconstructionNotifier` → `BuildingGoodsRecoveryService`), as after native explosions.
- **Native path (decompiled 1.1.2.8):** crater tiles through `ExplosionOutcomeGatherer.GetAffectedTerrainAndObjects` (unstable-core outcome: terrain only, objects in and on it, terrain physics); then `ITerrainPhysicsService.GetTerrainAndBlockObjectStack` over all removed terrain and objects (what stood on blast buildings, unsupported overhangs); then the `ExplosionService.DestroyEverythingAffected` order: `ITerrainService.UnsetTerrain`, `EntityService.Delete`, `DeconstructionParticleFactory.AddPausableParticles`. Bodies drop to `GetTerrainHeightBelow`, as `CharacterExploder.KillCharacter` does.
- **Flood:** `MeltdownCraterFloodFactor` 2 radioactive units per block each crater column's floor drops, at its open cell found before the destruction (removal only opens cells). Flat ground: 1,060 units, 530 fill the bowl and 530 spill over the rim (about 0.5 deep over the rest of the 12-tile area before evaporation). Removed ground gets no fallout deposit; the flood irradiates the new floor (keeps the result independent of when the radiation mirror sees the new floor; `RadiationSimulation.Reconcile` drops soil of a lowered floor).
- **Radiation rules unchanged:** same `IRadiationService` paths (water adds, soil, continuous source), so pumps stop, barrels contaminate, decon and the 0.5% cutoff work as before.
- **Saves:** all in one tick; `GameSaver` finishes the full tick, and `WaterSimulator` processes terrain modifications on `ForcedParallelTickFinished`, so a save holds the standing reactor or the finished meltdown. Fixed on the way: a core saved at heat 1 with coolant in stock used to cool a little on its first tick after load and never melt down (the service's pending list is not saved); a core that starts a tick at heat 1 now melts down. No new save data.
- **Dev mode:** `MeltdownDevModule`, "Deep Frontier: melt down selected reactor" (`ReactorCore.ForceMeltdown`: heat 1, the normal request).
- **Files:** `Scripts/Meltdown/MeltdownService.cs` (crater, blast, flood, circular `BuildingSite`), `MeltdownSpecs.cs` (4 keys), `ReactorCore.cs` (validation, `ForceMeltdown`), `WasteLeak.cs`, `MeltdownPanels.cs`, `MeltdownConfigurator.cs` (dev module); `tools/meltdown_content.mjs` (tuning, `craterProfile`, 4 texts), `tools/validate_meltdown_content.mjs`; generated reactor blueprint and `enUS.csv`; `docs/meltdown.md`; Timberborn skill `dependency-apis.md` ("Removing terrain the native way").
- **Checks:**
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check` in the mirror: 0 errors (3 existing warnings).
  - **Validator.** `validate_meltdown_content.mjs` adds: radii kill ≤ blast ≤ contamination, 1 ≤ rim ≤ kill and blast, depth ≥ 1, flood factor > 1; the crater covers all 14x11 footprint tiles and is identical in single and double precision; circular `BuildingSite`, `Within` for casualties, circular leak soil; the native call sequence in `MeltdownService`; the native contract decompiled from the plugin assemblies (`ExplosionService` order, `ExplosionOutcomeGatherer`, `CharacterExploder`, `ITerrainPhysicsService`, `BuildingSpec` → `Deconstructible`, deletion → goods recovery, `GetTerrainHeightBelow`); the dev module binding. Mutations caught: Chebyshev distance, crater down to z 0, particles dropped, all objects instead of `BuildingSpec`, rim 3 > kill radius, rim 1 (footprint corners uncut), flood factor 1.
  - **Generation.** Canonical generation in the mirror (others' uncommitted work reset to HEAD) changes exactly the reactor's `ReactorMeltdownSpec` (4 new keys) and 4 `enUS.csv` rows.
  - **validate_all.** `-Only` meltdown_content, priority_statuses, super_bots, radiation_content, building_descriptions, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, load_order, mod_dependencies, automation_content, vertical_slice_content, workforce_risks: 12/12. Full `validate_all -SkipBlender`: 70 of 72 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only); after the last `ReactorCore` change the `-Only` set again 12/12.
- **In game, to check (not run; the user is playing). Use a test save:**
  1. Dev mode on (Alt+Shift+Z). Build or pick a finished Nuclear Reactor on flat ground with paths, a platform, a small building and a tree 3–6 tiles from its walls, something 8 tiles away, and a pond or river within 12 tiles. Save.
  2. Select the reactor, open the dev panel and run "Deep Frontier: melt down selected reactor". Unpause.
  3. Within a tick: explosion sound, the notification "REACTOR MELTDOWN: the Nuclear Reactor exploded, leaving a crater, and destroyed N structures around it", the dialog (crater, "everything built within 6 tiles"). "Show the site" moves the camera.
  4. Crater: an oval bowl about 18 x 15 tiles, 4 deep in the middle, 1 at the rim, rim at most 2 tiles past the walls; terrain-removal dust. Everything built within 6 tiles is gone (outline rounded at the corners), the building 8 tiles away stands, the tree at 3–6 tiles stands (and dies of radiation). Goods from destroyed storage lie in recovered-goods piles.
  5. Flood: the crater fills with yellow radioactive water and it spills over the rim onto the ground around. Pumps on the nearby water stop ("radioactive water"), crops within 12 tiles die, and the ground tint's outline is round.
  6. Beavers within 2 tiles of the walls are dead, bodies on the ground (in the crater where it was dug); beavers 3–16 tiles away show Irradiation; bots there are unaffected.
  7. Save, reload: crater, destroyed buildings and the water stay; the source keeps pouring for 10 days. Then reload the save from step 1, pause, run the dev command, save while still paused, reload and unpause: the meltdown happens on the first tick.
  8. Leaks: a full Radioactive Waste Vault left for 24 h irradiates a rounded patch 3 tiles around it, not a square.

## Construction sites: every building shows a vanilla base, then a generated scaffold, instead of its finished model (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, construction-stage agent; stages built from a HEAD 9705995 view of mirror `constr`, generated and validated there; compile-checked; offline renders; not built or run in game)

- **Report (user):** "Some of our buildings look finished even when they are under construction."
- **Native mechanism** (decompiled 1.1.2.8 `ConstructionSiteProgressVisualizer`, `BuildingModel`, `ConstructionSite`, `ConstructionModeModel`):
  - The children of `#Unfinished` are stages, one shown at a time. Child 0, the base, shows until the first material or progress (`WasStarted`); then the last child whose `ProgressThresholds[i-1]` the build progress has reached.
  - Construction mode shows the finished model in greyscale.
  - **Vanilla buildings:** all 147 Iron Teeth buildings with a stage nest a vanilla `ConstructionBase<X>x<Y>`, sometimes smaller than the footprint and offset, plus one `ConstructionStage0` with thresholds `[0.0]`. The stage is a dark timber or BaseMetal frame on a Dirt patch, never the finished model.
  - Monuments use 2-4 progressive stages. Vanilla structural pieces (Platform, Dam, Levee, Stairs, MetalPlatform, Overhang, SuspensionBridge) have frame stages too, so no structural exemption is needed.
- **Audit (HEAD 9705995):** 92 Deep Frontier templates with a BuildingModelSpec.
  - **91 showed the finished model while unfinished.** 83 buildings and structures had `ConstructionStage0` as a byte copy of the finished model, with no base and thresholds `[]`. The 8 steel leg deck tools named the SteelDeck's copy.
  - **Only the Fisher Flag** had a native stage of its own (102 triangles, vanilla ConstructionBase1x1).
  - DeepOreSource is a map object, placed finished.
  - Per-template table: `outputs/construction-stages/audit.json`.
- **Fix:** generated at the pipeline level (docs/asset-pipeline.md "Construction stages"):
  - **Stage generator.** `tools/construction_stages.json` drives `construction_stage_massing.py`, `construction_stage_geometry.py` and `build_construction_stages.py`. Each building gets an outline frame in the vanilla stage atlas tiles on the native Dirt patch, cut at 60% of its main body over its own plan: timber, or steel on timber sills when it costs metal.
  - **Exports and binding.** The stages go through the official exporter to the tracked `outputs/construction-integration`. `tools/construction_stage_content.mjs` binds the largest fitting vanilla base plus the stage with `[0.0]` at the end of canonical generation.
  - **Families and exporters.** Eleven family generators call `unbindConstructionSite`, and the industrial and pit layouts stop writing stage colliders. The industrial and late-game exporters and the prototype exporter no longer copy the finished model to a stage. The 76 old stage copies in the integration folders are removed; `promote_style_exports.ps1` counts one mesh per template.
  - **Results.** 83 stages of 162-3,078 triangles (most 300-1,000; the old copies were 38-13,241). Total blueprint triangles 717,722 -> 408,359, with the construction ceilings in `triangle-budget.json` recorded at the new counts. The two tiny blocks go up, as vanilla Levee (26 -> 226) and Dam do: BrickMasonryBlock 38 -> 162 and ConcreteOverflowDam 132 -> 162.
  - **Unchanged.** Data changes only in each building's `#Unfinished` and thresholds and its stage mesh (checked against HEAD). The leg deck tools only reorder one key.
  - **Comments.** The comments in `PrefabMeshRelease.cs` and `RocketLaunchVisual.cs` no longer describe the stage as a copy.
- **Exemptions (two, with reasons in the config):**
  - `authored`: the Fisher Flag, whose native stage and base are already correct.
  - `previewOnly`: the SteelLegDeck tools, which SteelDeckPlacer never builds. Their unfinished child names the SteelDeck stage, now a scaffold.
- **Renders** (Cycles OptiX, RTX 2060, `render_map_object_views.py`), in `outputs/construction-stages/`:
  - `construction-stages-after.png`: all 83 stages;
  - `construction-stages-before-after.png`: 16 buildings, finished model versus stage;
  - `close/*-close-after.png`: StoneCrusher, SteelMill, Restaurant, NuclearReactor and SteelDeck3x3;
  - `vanilla/`: vanilla references (Smelter, LargeWarehouse, BotPartFactory, Centrifuge, MetalPlatform3x3, EfficientMine, Inventor, Dam stages and ConstructionBase3x3).
- **Checks:**
  - **Stage builder.** Every stage passes `zfight_audit.audit` with no visible conflict, plus footprint and height bounds, materials, UV tiles and the 4,000-triangle ceiling.
  - **New validator.** `exports-construction` covers all 92 templates, and its 4 mutations fail as they should.
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check`: 0 errors.
  - **Full validate_all** in the mirror: 76 of 78 pass. `no-game-assets` needs git; it passes on this commit's private index. `audit-item-uses` is report-only.
- **Incident:** while making the mirror show HEAD, a cleanup deleted the then-untracked `ModularWarehouse-` and `WarehouseModule-ReferenceStudy.blend` through the mirror's `Art/Source` junction.
  - They were regenerated at once with `render_lategame_studies.py --save-only` from unchanged sources (`sourceHash` equal). Their study, registration and native-assembly outputs are byte-identical, and they were unpacked (222,396 and 209,956 bytes).
  - The warehouse commit 9705995 then committed files of exactly those sizes.
- **In game, to check (not run):**
  1. Place a Stone Crusher (or any Deep Frontier building) without materials: staked-out ground with planks (the vanilla 3x3 base), not the building.
  2. Deliver the first materials: an open dark-timber frame on a dirt patch. A Steel Mill or Restaurant shows a steel frame. The finished model appears only when construction completes.
  3. Construction mode (hammer): unbuilt buildings show the finished model in grey, as vanilla.
  4. Structures:
     - **Concrete block and masonry blocks:** a 1x1 formwork cage.
     - **Concrete scaffold:** corner towers.
     - **Steel bridge and cantilever:** a frame along the span with the base at the anchor.
     - **Side platform:** a frame at deck level beside its anchor.
     - **Steel decks on legs:** each placed column and deck shows its own frame.
  5. Load a save with sites under construction: they show base or frame by their progress. Flipped and rotated sites keep the base on the building's ground.
  6. Fisher Flag: unchanged, with its landing and pole.

## Modular Warehouse: a core haulers use plus stackable Warehouse Modules, five slots with their own warehouse settings (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, datavault agent; mirror `datavault` on HEAD d1e5095 + this change; compile-checked; offline renders; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "a computerized storage warehouse ... BIG ... store as much as 20 unique items at a time (acts like 20 warehouses with their own settings?) ... maybe we can do 5 items. That way the menu system doesn't get clogged." Then (coordinator, the user's new direction): early game, no power, not computerized; MODULAR (one entrance building plus modules that add capacity and slots; "one entrance can handle multiple items"); STACKABLE (modules on each other and on the core). The first, powered 5x5 single-building version was reworked before anything was committed.
- **Native storage (decompiled 1.1.2.8):**
  - A warehouse is one `Stockpile` per entity: `StockpileInventoryInitializer` builds one `Inventory` of every good of the type (public input and output) with `SingleGoodAllower` as its only `IGoodDisallower`; `StockpilePriority` (Accept/Empty/Obtain/Supply) is per entity over `Emptiable`, `GoodObtainer` and `GoodSupplier`, whose behaviors read `GetComponent<Stockpile>().Inventory`.
  - Native code assumes one enabled `Inventory` per entity (`InventoryDebugFragment` `SingleOrDefault`), so several goods share one inventory: `Inventory.LimitedAmount(good) = min(disallower.AllowedAmount(good), allowed amount)`; stock above it is unwanted stock (rechecked on `DisallowedGoodsChanged`), carried out by laborers (`RemoveUnwantedStockLaborBehavior`, district registry) and haulers (`UnwantedStockHaulBehaviorProvider`).
  - `DistrictInventoryPicker.InventoryIsTaking` reads the inventory's `IInventoryValidator` (native `Emptiable`); `CarryRootBehavior.CompleteDelivery` rechecks capacity on arrival, so a lowered limit is safe mid-delivery. `CreateWithUnlimitedCapacity` (int.MaxValue) is the native way to let limits come from elsewhere (`SimpleOutputInventoryInitializer`, `GoodStackInventoryInitializer`).
  - Expanded Storage 1.0.1's stackable modules are independent single-good stockpiles that only stack physically (top layer `Stackable: BlockObject`); no mod pools storage across buildings. Deleting a building with an inventory recovers its goods (`RecoverableGoodProvider`).
- **Design:**
  - **Modular Warehouse** (core, `Buildings/Storage/ModularWarehouse`): 3x3x2, door in front, flat plank deck (all 9 top cells Stackable), 400 science (T1), no power or workers, 100 Plank + 60 Log + 15 Gear. It holds the network's one native inventory. No Harmony.
  - **Warehouse Module** (`Buildings/Storage/WarehouseModule`): 3x3x1, no door (no Entrance, BuildingAccessible or Enterable: `lategame_building_content.mjs` doorless option, renderer `DOORLESS`), flat deck (all 9 top cells Stackable, ground cells GroundOrStackable), 450 science, 80 Plank + 60 Log + 5 Gear.
  - **Attachment rule:** a finished module is attached when it touches the finished core or an attached module face to face (side by side, or stacked on or under it; diagonal contact does not count). The search runs over native block occupancy (`IBlockService.GetObjectsAt` across the six faces of each occupied cell) from every core at once, so a module touching two networks joins the nearer core (ties: the lower entity id). `ModularWarehouseNetwork` (singleton) reruns on the tick after any core or module enters or leaves its finished state, and in `PostLoad`.
  - **Slots and capacity:** open slots = 1 + attached modules, at most 5 (the user's five; the panel stays at five native warehouse boxes at most); capacity = 600 (core) + 600 per module, shared equally by the open slots. Each slot has one good (not in two slots), the native Accept / Obtain / Supply / Empty setting and its share. A core with four modules holds 5 x 600; with nine, 5 x 1,200. A module has the vanilla Large Warehouse's density (600 in 9 cells; 1,200 in 18).
  - **Boxed goods only**, like the vanilla warehouses (`GoodType` Box): piles and liquids have their own native buildings and visualizers.
  - **Leftovers and removal:** a slot switched to another good keeps the old stock as leftovers (native unwanted stock) while the new good fills the rest of the slot. Removing a module lowers the capacity: stock above the new shares, and the good of a slot that closes (cleared), become native unwanted stock and are carried out; nothing is lost (goods never sit in a module). The core saves the network it last saw (`Modules`, `NetworkCapacity`), so a load starts from it before `PostLoad` recomputes it.
  - **Costs:** the size rule alone gives these small early buildings 15-20 Plank, 10 Log and 5 Gear; `lategameGates` adds vanilla-warehouse pricing for their capacity (documented in `building_construction_costs.json` and docs/progression.md "Modular Warehouse").
  - **Panel:** one line "Modules: N. Capacity: X", then one native warehouse box per open slot (`Game/EntityPanel/StockpileInventoryFragment`: good button with the native picker rebuilt from public services, unselect, stock / share bar, leftover row) with the native four-way priority toggle (`stockpile-priority-toggle__icon--*`, `StockpilePriority.*` texts). An unattached module shows the native `UnconnectedBuilding` status icon ("Not attached to a Modular Warehouse").
  - **Hauling:** Obtain/Supply haul behaviors and providers mirror the native ones per slot (never from or to the warehouse itself, nor an obtaining or supplying native stockpile or slot of the same good). Native `Emptiable`, `EmptyInventoriesWorkplaceBehavior`, `RemoveUnwantedStockWorkplaceBehavior`, `InventoryNeedBehavior` and (through Emptiable) `HaulCandidate` are decorated on the core. Conveyor Belts v2 links to it as to any warehouse (native `Inventories`, public input and output).
- **Files:** `src/DeepFrontier/Scripts/ModularStorage/` (ModularWarehouse, WarehouseModule with the network, hauling, panel, good selection box, configurator); `tools/create_frontier_expansion_content.mjs` (makeModularWarehouse, makeWarehouseModule, text rows), `tools/frontier_progression.json` (`modularWarehouse`), `tools/lategame_building_content.mjs` (doorless), `tools/validate_modular_warehouse.mjs` (new); the studies `tools/asset-pipeline/lategame_ModularWarehouse.py` and `lategame_WarehouseModule.py` with their `Art/Source/*-ReferenceStudy.blend` (223 and 210 KB, unpacked), `render_lategame_studies.py` and `export_lategame_models.py` (doorless); registries (batches, cost tiers and gates, asset catalog, triangle budget, material mix and context, roof decks, connection faces, sounds, automation `none`, tool groups after the Large Warehouse, the single-inventory and vertical-slice validators); icons `Art/Icons/ModularWarehouse.svg` and `WarehouseModule.svg`; the `AllBuildingsAcceptance.cs` slot probe.
- **Checks:**
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check`: 0 errors; the new files also compile against the shipped (non-publicized) assemblies (public API only).
  - **Validator.** `validate_modular_warehouse.mjs` passes: catalog, both generated blueprints, runtime source, and the native contract decompiled from the plugin assemblies (inventory limits and unwanted stock, unlimited inventories, IInventoryValidator, the Emptiable decorators, StockpileInventoryInitializer, the Obtain/Supply filters, laborer unwanted-stock removal, the delivery recheck, bounds-safe `GetObjectsAt`, goods recovery, the priority toggle). Mutations caught: Empty mode removed, the downward face dropped from the search, Emptiable removed, an unstackable module top, slots no longer closing.
  - **Generation.** Canonical generation in the mirror changes exactly the two new building folders, their template-collection lines, the Storage native order and eight localization rows, plus the registries listed above.
  - **validate_all.** `-Only` 28 checks (modular_warehouse, single_inventory, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, automation_content, vertical_slice_content, building_descriptions, building_sounds, tool_order, tool_groups, power_rule, audit-progression, connection_faces, roof_decks, flat_roof_platforms, roof_hardpoints, freed cells, status_icon_anchors, load_order, building_entrances, construction_materials, exports-lategame, model-bindings, triangle-budget, animations, audit-building-assets): 28/28. Full `validate_all` with the Blender source checks (run in four parts: one single run stopped after exports-pit without an error): 75 of 77 pass; `no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only (0 of 51 goods need work). `priority_statuses` first flagged the emptying status as a priority status; it is now a normal status.
  - **Art.** z-fight audit: 0 visible conflicts (frames 1, 31, 61, 91) for both; hidden geometry 43% and 45%, 0 culled faces exposed; 2,182 and 1,342 triangles ('small' class).
- **Renders (Cycles, RTX 2060, OptiX):** `outputs/lategame-studies/ModularWarehouse-contact-sheet.png` (both studies), `ModularWarehouse-assembly.png` (a core with two modules stacked on it and one beside it), and each study's front, front-right, rear, top-angle and culled views.
- **In game, to check (not run; the user was playing):**
  1. Storage menu: Modular Warehouse and Warehouse Module right after the Large Warehouse (400 and 450 science).
  2. Build a Modular Warehouse: its panel shows "Modules: 0. Capacity: 600" and one warehouse box. Pick Logs and keep Accept: haulers bring logs up to 600.
  3. Build a module beside it and one on its roof (a stair or path up so builders reach it): once both finish, the panel shows "Modules: 2. Capacity: 1800" and three boxes of 600. A module standing alone shows the "Not attached" status; place one touching the core or an attached module and the status clears.
  4. Stack two more modules on the first: five boxes. More modules raise each box's capacity (1,200 each with nine).
  5. With a hauling post: Obtain makes haulers fetch the slot's good from other storage; Supply makes them take it to other storage; Empty has workers carry it out and shows the "Emptying" status.
  6. Switch a filled slot to another good: the old good shows as a leftover row and is carried out while the new good fills the rest.
  7. Delete a module while all five slots are full: the last slot closes, and its goods and the excess are carried to other storage; no goods disappear.
  8. Save and reload: slots, modes, leftovers and capacities are unchanged. Copying settings from one Modular Warehouse to another copies the open slots' goods and modes.


## Fix: planters replanted irradiated ground forever; they now skip it like contaminated soil (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, replant agent; mirror `replant` on HEAD 6a9f15b + this change; compile-checked; startup verification and patch run in the game's Mono against the shipped DLLs; not built or run in game)

- **User report:** "The beavers try to replace the dead crops on irradiated ground and it immediately dies, then the beaver tries replacing it again, so it gets stuck in a cycle, never moving on to good ground."
- **Cause (decompiled 1.1.2.8, `Timberborn.Planting`, `Cutting`):** every planter (FarmHouse, Forester and their variants) picks its spot with `PlantingSpotFinder.FindClosest`, which keeps a marked spot only if the private `CanPlantAt` passes: prioritized plantable, `PlantingSoilValidator.Validate` (moisture, `!ISoilContaminationService.SoilIsContaminated`), the building's `IPlantingSpotValidator`, floodable water depth, `SpawnValidationService.IsUnobstructed`. Irradiated ground passed all of them. Crops are `RemoveOnCut`, so a dead crop is overridable (`EmptyDeadNaturalResourceOverrider`) and the farmer planted straight over it; `IrradiatedGroundDeath.PostInitializeEntity` killed the new crop; the spot was free again.
- **Native rule copied:** contaminated or dry soil is refused in `CanPlantAt`, while the planting tool (`PlantingAreaValidator`) and the "nothing to do" status ignore soil. Marks stay and planting resumes by itself when the soil recovers.
- **Fix (`Scripts/Radiation/IrradiatedPlantingSpots.cs`):** one Harmony postfix on `PlantingSpotFinder.CanPlantAt` refuses a spot every native test accepted when `RadiationSimulation.IsIrradiatedGround` is true, the call that kills a plant planted there (one threshold, soil and bank layer, tide water included). The rule is the component `IrradiatedPlantingSpots`, decorated on `PlantablePrioritizer` (where native `PlantingSpotFinder` is decorated) with `RadiationSimulation` injected; the postfix gets it from the planter entity. No static state and no `Load` dependency. The planting warning now reads "... die immediately. Planters skip that ground until it is clean."
- **Why Harmony:** the soil rule has no extension point. `PlantingSoilValidator` is internal with a non-virtual `Validate`; `IPlantingSpotValidator` is read with `GetComponent`, which throws when a second component implements it (FarmHouse and Forester already do); `ISoilContaminationService` is the badwater field every native system reads; `ISpawnValidator` serves natural spreading only.
- **Self-check at mod start** (`IrradiatedPlantingNative.Verify`, throws on a changed game): `CanPlantAt(PlantingSpot, PlantableSpec) : bool` exists and is above the Mono inline limit (120 IL bytes); it calls `PlantingSoilValidator.Validate`, which calls `SoilIsContaminated` and `SoilIsMoist`; `GetClosestOrDefault` calls it; `PlanterWorkplaceBehavior.Decide` and `PlantBehavior.ReserveCoordinates` call `FindClosest`; `PlantingConfigurator` decorates `PlantingSpotFinder` on `PlantablePrioritizer`.
- **Dead plants:** plants killed while growing stay dead in place, as after a badwater death, and are planted over only once the ground is clean (crops overridable; a forester replanting dead trees demolishes them only after `FindClosest` accepted the spot). A planter that reserved a spot before it turned irradiated plants it once (native planters do not re-check a reservation); that plant dies and the spot is skipped afterwards.
- **Checks:**
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check` in the mirror: 0 errors, 0 warnings.
  - **Mono harness** (game's Mono 6.13, shipped 1.1.2.8 DLLs, Harmony 2.4.1): `Verify` passes on the private `CanPlantAt` (120 IL bytes); the patch class applies with every parameter injection resolved (one postfix on `CanPlantAt`); `IrradiatedPlantingSpots` loads.
  - **Validator.** `validate_radiation_content.mjs` checks the decorator and binding, that the rule is `!_simulation.IsIrradiatedGround(coordinates)` (the death test), the editor guard, Verify before patching, what Verify checks, the postfix (only refuses accepted spots, entity component, throws without it), no static state or `Load`, the warning text, and the native contract decompiled from the plugin assemblies (`CanPlantAt` calls the soil rule, the soil rule refuses contaminated soil, single `IPlantingSpotValidator`, the decorator, both `FindClosest` callers, the planting tool without a soil test). Mutations caught: postfix without the early return, a second threshold, the decorator removed, Harmony outside the guard, a static `Current`, Verify skipped, the inline check removed.
  - **Generation.** Canonical generation in the mirror leaves Data unchanged apart from the warning row, which matches `radiation_content.mjs`.
  - **validate_all.** `-Only` radiation_content, editor_guards, component_lifecycle, load_order, contamination_consumers, vertical_slice_content, radioactive_tide_content, mod_dependencies, building_descriptions: 9/9. Full `validate_all -SkipBlender`: 68 of 70 pass (`exports-roof_hardpoint` failed at first only on another agent's uncommitted Modular Warehouse roof records synced into the mirror, and passes on HEAD + this change; `no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only).
- **In game, to check (not run):**
  1. Load a save with irradiated (yellow-green) ground inside a farmhouse's marked field and dead crops on it. The farmers plant and harvest the clean cells and leave the irradiated cells and their dead crops alone; no crop appears and dies there. `Player.log` has "[DeepFrontier] Irradiated planting patch applied".
  2. Mark new crops on irradiated ground: the marks are accepted, the tooltip reads "... die immediately. Planters skip that ground until it is clean.", and nothing is planted there.
  3. Same with a forester's tree marks on irradiated ground, also with "Replant dead trees" on: no planting, no demolition of dead trees there.
  4. Rinse the ground (clean water over it for about 5 game hours, or let banks clear): when the tint goes, the farmers plant over the dead crops by themselves and the crops grow.
  5. During a Radioactive Tide, fields beside tide water stop being replanted; after the tide and rinsing, planting resumes.

## Fix: new game crashed in the Reactor Bot worker-type toggle; dev-mode Reactor Bot generator (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, bot-light agent; mirror `botwork` on HEAD 283306f (rebased on b4340b9, menu order only) + this change; compile-checked; patches and new types run in the game's Mono against the shipped DLLs; not built or run in game)

- **Crash (coordinator, build 259):** starting a new game threw "ReactorBotWorkplaceUi is not loaded" in `DistrictCenterFragmentPostfix` (`EntityPanel.Load` -> `InitializeModules` -> `AddFragments` -> `DistrictCenterFragment.InitializeFragment`).
  - **Cause (decompiled 1.1.2.8):** `SingletonLifecycleService.LoadAll` calls every singleton's `Load`, then loads the entities, then calls every `PostLoad`, with no order between unrelated singletons. `EntityPanel.Load` builds every entity-panel fragment there. My postfixes read the static `ReactorBotWorkplaceUi.Current` (set in its `Load`) and tinted the icon with `ReactorBotWorkplaceSettings.WorkplaceColor` (resolved in its `Load`) at that moment. Loading a save happened to load them first.
- **Fix (no null guards):**
  - **Statics removed.** No static singleton access remains: `ReactorBotWorkplaceUi.Current` and `ReactorBotWorkplaceRegistry.Current` are gone, and neither class has a `Load` any more.
  - **Toggle callbacks.** The toggle postfixes touch no Deep Frontier singleton. Every item callback reaches the UI helper through the shown entity's own component (`ReactorBotWorkplace.Ui`, `ReactorBotDistrictDefault.Ui`, constructor-injected). Those callbacks run only when a panel is shown, updated or clicked, after loading.
  - **Icon tint.** The third icon is tinted on the toggle's first update (`LazyTint`).
  - **Workplace order.** The native-assigner postfixes read the mode from the workplace's `ReactorBotWorkplace` component instead of a static registry.
  - **Audit.** The other patches run only after loading: the unemployed-pool prefix reads entity state, the workplace-order postfixes run on ticks, and the district-default postfix runs at finished-state entry or district events, when entities have loaded after every singleton `Load`.
- **Dev-mode Reactor Bot generator** (user). The native pattern (decompiled `Timberborn.BotsUI`): `BotGeneratorTool` is an `IDevModeTool` that calls `BotFactory.Create(position)` on the clicked tile, ten times with SpawnManyCharacters held; `BotGeneratorButton` is a red groupless bottom-bar button at left-section order 80.
  - **Tool and button.** `ReactorBotGeneratorTool` and `ReactorBotGeneratorButton` (`Scripts/SuperBots/ReactorBotGenerator.cs`) copy them: order 81, the native BotGeneratorTool image tinted with the Reactor Bot colour, applied in `PostLoad`, since the bottom bar collects its elements during singleton loading.
  - **Birth path.** Each bot is born as a Reactor Bot through the path the Reactor bot recipe uses: `ReactorBotAssemblyLine.ExpectSpawn`, then native `BotFactory.Create`, then `ReactorBot.InitializeEntity` takes the birth (Reactor state, reactor needs full), then `Settle` throws if no bot took it.
  - **Shared file.** `ReactorBotAssembly.cs` (re-read before editing; unchanged since cb8fb87): the line now accepts an assembler birth (needs the native `BotManufacturedEvent` birth init) or a generator birth (no birth init, as `BotFactory.Create(position)` makes).
- **Checks:**
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check`: 0 errors.
  - **Validator.** `validate_reactor_bot_workplaces.mjs` passes. It now also checks the load-order rule: no static `Current` in these files, and in the two toggle postfixes every use of the UI helper or the tint sits in an item callback. It also checks the generator (dev-mode tool, the birth call order, the `PostLoad` tint, button order and image), the assembly line's two birth kinds, and the native contract: the `LoadAll` order, `BotGeneratorTool` and its bottom-bar order 80, `BotFactory.Create(position)` adding no birth init, `BotManufactory`'s `BotManufacturedEvent`, and the tool button image.
  - **Mutations.** It fails on a tint at initialization, a static `Current`, a button tint before `PostLoad`, a spawn without the Reactor Bot birth, and the birth check removed.
  - **Mono harness** (game's Mono 6.13, shipped DLLs): the six methods verify and patch; the new types load; `Settle` without a bot throws.
  - **validate_all.** `-Only` reactor_bot_workplaces, super_bots, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, automation_content, vertical_slice_content, bot_upkeep: 7/7. Full `validate_all -SkipBlender`: 68 of 70 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only).
- **In game, to check (not run):**
  1. **New game.** It starts (the build 259 crash).
  2. **District Center.** Its panel shows Beaver | Bot | Reactor Bot, the third icon tinted green after the panel opens.
  3. **Workplace panel and batch window.** Both show three buttons that work.
  4. **Load a save.** Same as above.
  5. **Dev mode.** A red button with a green bot icon sits right of the bot generator.
     - A click spawns a Reactor Bot: yellow eyes, dark plating, panel "Reactor Bot", reactor charge, coolant and plating full.
     - With the many-characters key held, it spawns ten.
     - The native bot generator still spawns ordinary bots.

## Menu order: related tools stand together (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, tool-order agent; generated and validated in mirror `toolorder` on HEAD 283306f; not built or run in game)

- **Player feedback (screenshot):** the Tubeway Station stood apart from the Tubeways group in Paths; Explosives Factory, Dynamite and Tunnel were split by the Masonry and concrete group in Landscaping.

The other menus were reviewed for split related tools; none needed a change (the Fish Hatchery feeds both the Greenhouse and the Fishery Works, so it stays in science order).
