## Reactor Bots: 500% working speed, and radioactive leaks when overheated or unplated (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, botleak agent; mirror `botleak` on HEAD e5e0f6e + this change; compile-checked; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "Can we make the nuclear bots work at 500% working speed base? If they overheat or go unmaintained for too long then they can leak radioactive water on the ground."
- **Working speed, native mechanism (decompiled 1.1.2.8):**
  - `Worker.Tick` sets `WorkingSpeedMultiplier = BonusManager.Multiplier("WorkingSpeed")` and passes it to the animator's `WorkingSpeed` float, which the bot's Working/Building states use as their `SpeedModifier`.
  - `ProduceExecutor` (`IncreaseProductionProgress(dt x WorkingSpeedMultiplier)`), `BuildExecutor` (`IncreaseBuildTime`), `PlantExecutor` and `WorkAtReservableExecutor` (cutting, gathering, harvesting, scavenging, demolition: duration / multiplier) apply it; so does Deep Frontier's `FisherCatchExecutor`.
  - `BonusManager` starts every bonus at 1, adds deltas and clamps to the `BonusTypeSpec`: `BonusType.WorkingSpeed` is 0.05..1000, so x5 needs no patch (unlike movement speed, clamped at 2.0). Wellbeing tiers add their own deltas (bots: +0.65 at wellbeing 0, +1.1 at 1, +1.7 at 2, then +0.2 per point), and the wellbeing panel shows the sum minus 1.
- **Working speed, change:** `upgrade.workingSpeedMultiplier` 5 in `tools/super_bots.json` becomes `FrontierReactorBotSettingsSpec.WorkingSpeedMultiplier`. `ReactorBot.ApplyUpgrade` adds the native delta `5 - 1` on `WorkingSpeed` beside the carrying bonus, so the base goes from 100% to 500%. `ReactorBotSettings.Load` throws if the bonus type's maximum is below it.
  - Panel rows: "5 times the load, 4 times the movement speed, 5 times the working speed and 10 times the lifespan" (retrofit), and "5 times the load, 4 times the movement speed and 5 times the working speed" (Reactor Bot).
- **Leaks (`src/DeepFrontier/Scripts/SuperBots/ReactorBotLeak.cs`, own configurator, decorated on `ReactorBot`, no Harmony):**
  - *Overheated:* the coolant need in its native critical state (points at 0, the native overheating status) for more than `leak.overheatHours` 6.
  - *Unmaintained:* the plating need exhausted (`ReactorBot.IsPlated` false) for more than `leak.unmaintainedHours` 24. Lifespan wear is not a condition: every bot ages however well it is kept, and an unplated Reactor Bot already wears 5 times faster.
  - While either has passed its threshold: `IRadiationService.AddRadioactiveWater` of `leak.volumePerHour` 0.02 x `leak.pulseHours` 1, one pulse at once and then hourly. That is 0.48 per day, 40% of a full waste vault's or blocked refinery's leak. It stops when both are fixed, and each condition's hours restart from 0.
  - *Where:* the bot's cell, or the doorstep of the building it is hidden in (the building's blocks keep water out). `TryFindOpenCell` then puts the water on the floor of the open column there (ground, platform or roof top, or the water surface), or at the lowest open cell above a closed cell, as waste leaks do.
  - No status or notification of its own.
  - Saved per bot (`DeepFrontier.ReactorBotLeak`: `OverheatedHours`, `UnplatedHours`, `NextPulseHours`).
  - Settings: `FrontierReactorBotLeakSpec` (`CoolantNeedId`, `OverheatHours`, `UnmaintainedHours`, `VolumePerHour`, `PulseHours`) in the same `Configurations/DeepFrontierReactorBots.blueprint.json`. `ReactorBotLeakSettings` requires the coolant need to be a critical reactor need.
  - docs/radiation.md "Reactor Bot leaks".
- **Checks:**
  - Canonical generation in the mirror changes exactly the settings blueprint and the two panel rows.
  - `validate_super_bots` now covers:
    - `workingSpeedMultiplier` > 1 and within the vanilla `BonusType.WorkingSpeed` clamp;
    - the `leak` values, and the leak rate below both waste buildings' leaks;
    - the leak record matching the generator;
    - `ReactorBot` adding the delta;
    - `ReactorBotLeak` using the critical state, `IsPlated`, `TryFindOpenCell`, `AddRadioactiveWater`, the doorstep, saving its hours, adding no status or notification, and not subscribing in `Awake`;
    - the native contract, decompiled from the plugin assemblies: `Worker`, `ProduceExecutor`, `BuildExecutor`, `BonusManager` and `Need.IsInCriticalState`.
  - Mutation run: 9 seeded faults (working speed 1, a missing `- 1`, a renamed leak field, soil instead of water, the entrance instead of the doorstep, a `StatusToggle`, an edited settings file, and 0.06 per hour against both waste buildings) all reported.
  - `tools/compile-check`: 0 errors (3 earlier warnings).
  - `validate_all -Only` super_bots, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, bot_upkeep, automation_content, reactor_bot_workplaces, radiation_content, meltdown_content, workforce_risks, vertical_slice_content, load_order, deep_frontier_manifest: 12/12.
  - Full `validate_all -SkipBlender`: 68 of 70 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only, unchanged).
- **In game, to check (not run; the user was playing):**
  1. Load a save with a Reactor Bot (or, in dev mode, finish a Reactor bot recipe at the Bot Assembler). Its wellbeing panel shows Working speed 400 points above an ordinary bot's at the same wellbeing (+465% against +65% at wellbeing 0). Progress therefore runs (5 + t) / (1 + t) times an ordinary bot's, where t is the wellbeing delta: about 3.4 times at wellbeing 0, 5 times with no wellbeing bonus. Its work animation is fast.
  2. Empty the district of reactor coolant water and cryocoolant. In dev mode, open the bot's Wellbeing panel, press Edit, and lower Coolant to 0 with the minus button (0.2 per click). The native "Reactor Bot is overheating" status appears. About 6 game hours later a small yellow puddle appears under the bot, or at the doorstep of the building it is in, then one every hour; the ground there turns yellow-green.
  3. Restock coolant: once the bot drinks, the leaking stops.
  4. Lower Steel plating to 0 with no steel or alloy stored: after 24 h it leaks the same way; restocking stops it.
  5. Let a leaking bot stand in a pump's water: the pump refuses ("radioactive" share at the intake).
  6. Save while it leaks (and during the grace hours) and reload: the hours carry on (no reset to 0).
  7. A leaking bot on a platform or roof: the water lands there and runs off the edge.

## Reactor Bot glow: neon-yellow eyes and joint seams in an HDR emission map (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, industrial-bot agent; mirror `indbot` on HEAD 05a5b83 + this change; compile-checked; offline renders; not built or run in game)

- **User, on the cb8fb87 renders:** "need neon yellow, bright yellow, and maybe we can do the same in between the joints"; "like a glow"; "we don't need to change the shader though".
- **Glow colour:** 0.93/1.0/0.0 (sRGB 237/255/0), the radiation yellow's hue (`RadiationMaterials.WaterTintColor` 0.86/0.92/0.08, hue 64.3 degrees) at full saturation and value (was the tint itself). `validate_super_bots` now requires the glow hue within 5 degrees of the radiation yellow, a channel at 1 and one at 0, and the illumination colour equal to it (an orange or a dull yellow fails).
- **Glow, not paint:** the emission map is now a linear `RGBAHalf` texture at half resolution (1024 for the 2048 maps, mipmapped, about 11 MB): linear glow colour x `GlowIntensity` 2.0 x glow mask, so the eyes and seams reach 2 (x2 with the shader's bloom, against the vanilla cyan eye's about 0.7 x 2), which an 8-bit map cannot hold. The base maps (plated and unplated, DXT-compressed) are painted the glow colour under it. The game's BotURP shader and material are untouched; only the textures set through `CharacterMaterialModifier.SetTexture` change.
- **Joint seams:** the body mesh is read at runtime (Timbermesh `StaticMeshBuilder`, decompiled 1.1.2.8: `new Mesh()`, never made non-readable, not `Optimize()`d when vertex-animated, so the bind pose in model space with uv0). `ReactorBotSkin` splits it into welded connected pieces (43 on the Iron Teeth bot; its UVs share no texels), and near each declared joint pivot glows the surface within `GlowRim` 0.025 of another piece: (1 - d / rim)^2 x clamp01(2 (1 - |p - pivot| / radius)) x `GlowJointGain` 1.4, rasterised in UV at half resolution (closest point on triangle, Ericson). Joints (`tools/super_bots.json` `glow.joints`, bind-pose pivots measured on the model, mirrored left/right): Neck 0.07, Shoulder 0.08, Elbow 0.07, Hip 0.07, Knee 0.08, Ankle 0.06, TailRoot 0.07 (12 with the mirrors). A pivot with no neighbouring piece adds nothing.
- **Settings:** `EyeColor` -> `GlowColor`, `GlowIntensity`, `GlowRim`, `GlowJointGain`, `GlowJoints` (`FrontierReactorBotGlowJointSpec` Name, Pivot, Radius); `upgrade.eyeColor` now fails the catalog check. Generation changes only the settings blueprint and the light colour.
- **Checks:** Targeted `validate_all -Only` super_bots, bot_upkeep, automation_content, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, audit-progression 6/6; full `validate_all -SkipBlender` 68 of 70 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` report-only, unchanged); `tools/compile-check` 0 errors (3 earlier warnings).
- **Offline renders** (game model and textures read at render time, never committed; Cycles on the RTX 2060 with OptiX, BotURP emulated: base x tint, emission map x 2, the HDR glow as mask x linear colour x 2.0 x 2): `outputs/reactor-bot-skin/reactor-bot-glow-sheet.png`, day and night: ordinary bot | Reactor Bot plated | unplated.
- **In game, to check:** a Reactor Bot's eyes and joint seams glow neon yellow by day and bloom at night (and while it walks and works: the seams are painted in the bind pose and move with the parts); ordinary bots unchanged. The first Reactor Bot of a session builds the textures once (a short hitch).

## Reactor Bot skin: radioactive-yellow eyes and blackened plating from the bot's own textures; the recipe is "Reactor bot" (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, industrial-bot agent; mirror `indbot` on HEAD 1ffae1a + this change; compile-checked; offline renders; not built or run in game)

- **User (build 252):** "new bots need the radioactive yellow eyes... the new bots also need some black on them... dark tones on them to make them really stand out"; and "since we named them reactor bots, let's make the recipe be reactor bot, not industrial bot (title)".
- **Why the eyes stayed cyan (source):** the bot body is one `Shader Graphs/BotURP` material (`Bot.IronTeeth`, read from `resources.assets` with UnityPy: `_BaseMap` BotIronTeeth_2K_D, `_BaseMap_1` BotIronTeeth_2K_E). The official `BotURP.shadergraph` (modding tools, `Resources/Shaders`) computes `BaseColor = lerp(1, _TintColor, _TintEnabled) * _BaseMap * _BaseColor` and `Emission = _BaseMap_1 * bloom + _EmissionColor + 0.35 * _TintColor * _TintEnabled`, and has no `_LightingColor`: the `MaterialColorer` illumination colour of 22e5c8d (as vanilla `BotIlluminationController` sets blue) never reached the body. The eye cyan is painted in `_BaseMap` and baked into `_BaseMap_1`, which is black elsewhere. The steel `CharacterTint` multiplied the body by 0.62-0.72 and added 0.22-0.25 glow, so the bot looked washed out, not plated (offline render: lighter than vanilla, glowing at night).
- **Skin (`Scripts/SuperBots/ReactorBotSkin.cs`):** a texture set made at runtime from the bot material's own maps (GPU blit + `ReadPixels`, so no game texture ships): eye mask = emission brightness over its peak (0 at or below 4/255); emission = eye colour x mask; unplated base = `_BaseMap` with the eye blended to the eye colour; plated base = dark plating `(c/255)^1.8 x 0.57 x (0.88, 0.92, 1.0)` per channel under the same eye. Each 2048 texture is mipmapped, runtime-compressed and made non-readable; one set per source map pair, shared by all Reactor Bots, destroyed on unload. `ReactorBot` sets the emission map and the plated or unplated base through native `CharacterMaterialModifier.SetTexture` (the `CharacterTextureSetter` mechanism; the bot template has no `CharacterTextureSetterSpec`), and swaps the base when the plating need changes. Eye colour 0.86/0.92/0.08 (sRGB 219/235/20), the radiation yellow `RadiationMaterials.WaterTintColor`; the illumination colour is the same yellow. Light metal panels (230) go to about 106/111/121, mid tones (128) to about 37/39/42.
- **Removed:** the steel `CharacterTint` and its Harmony postfix on `CitizenTint.UpdateTint` (ReactorBotPatches now patches speed and lifespan only); `FrontierReactorBotSettingsSpec.Tint` -> `EyeColor`, `DarkPlatingGamma`, `DarkPlatingScale`, `DarkPlatingColor` (`tools/super_bots.json` `eyeColor`, `darkPlating`; `upgrade.tint` now fails the catalog check). A colour-named Reactor Bot keeps its native CitizenTint over the skin.
- **Recipe title:** `botAssembly.displayName` "Industrial bot" -> "Reactor bot"; the id stays `IndustrialBot` (native `Manufactory.Load` also matches `BackwardCompatibleIds`, so a renamed id would have been save-safe; only the title was asked for). Bot Assembler description (143 characters): "Assembles bots from a chassis, four limbs and a head. Reactor bots take a reinforced bot frame and a reactor power cell instead of the chassis." Comments, docs/progression.md and CHANGELOG follow.
- **Checks:** `validate_super_bots`: eye and illumination colours = `RadiationMaterials.WaterTintColor`; `ReactorBotSkin` property names = the BotURP graph's `_BaseMap` / `_BaseMap_1` overrides; no `CharacterTextureSetterSpec` on the vanilla bot; `ReactorBot` sets the emission map and swaps the plating base (each fails on a wrong value). Generation changes only the settings blueprint, the light colour and the two Bot Assembler rows; `material_costs --check` clean. Targeted `validate_all -Only` super_bots, bot_upkeep, automation_content, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, building_descriptions, audit-progression, tool_order, tool_groups, workforce_risks 10/10; full `validate_all -SkipBlender` 67 of 69 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` report-only, unchanged); `tools/compile-check` 0 errors (3 earlier warnings).
- **Offline renders** (game model and textures read at render time, never committed; Blender Cycles on the RTX 2060 with OptiX, BotURP emulated: base x tint, emission x 2 + 0.35 x tint): `outputs/reactor-bot-skin/reactor-bot-skin-sheet.png` (day and night: ordinary bot with cyan eyes; Reactor Bot plated, near-black gunmetal with radioactive-yellow eyes; unplated, the vanilla light body with yellow eyes).
- **In game, to check:** a Reactor Bot (assembled or retrofitted, and one loaded from a save) has yellow eyes and a dark body by day and night; one out of plating shows the light body with yellow eyes, dark again after it takes steel; ordinary bots unchanged; the Bot Assembler dropdown shows "Reactor bot"; a save made with the assembler on the old recipe loads with "Reactor bot" selected. First Reactor Bot in a session: the textures are built once (expect a short hitch).

## Reactor Bot workplaces: a third worker type (Beaver | Bot | Reactor Bot), exclusive, at any building (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, bot-light agent; mirror `botwork` on HEAD e06b3f4 (rebased on 725574a, which changed only the release files) + this change; compile-checked; patches verified in the game's Mono against the shipped DLLs; not built or run in game)

- **User** (Option 1 of `docs/worker-types-study.md`, with the answers to its decision points):
  1. Reactor Bots work only at Reactor Bot workplaces, and Bot workplaces take ordinary bots only.
  2. The Reactor Bot option costs 3x the workplace's bot unlock.
  3. Reactor Bots are allowed at ANY building.
  4. The District Center's default gets the third option.
- **Design and evidence** (decompiled 1.1.2.8): `docs/worker-types-study.md` "What was built". In short:
  - **The mode.** A workplace set to Reactor Bots keeps the native type Bot and a saved Deep Frontier flag (`ReactorBotWorkplace`, decorated on every `WorkplaceWorkerType`).
    - On beaver-only workplaces, or where bots are still locked, the Bot type is set without the native allowed/unlocked checks (`NativeWorkerTypeAccess`: release the workers, set the type, raise `WorkerTypeChanged`, mark it chosen).
    - Turning the mode off puts back a type the workplace may natively have.
  - **Staffing.** `ReactorBotStaffing` (the `SpecialistStaffing` pattern) staffs Reactor Bot workplaces with Reactor Bots of the district, by priority, and releases Reactor Bots from every other workplace.
  - **Specialists.** The Reactor Bot mode wins: `SpecialistStaffing` skips such a workplace and clears its statuses, and the panels say Reactor Bots work there. Reason: the user's "ANY building"; the specialist rule is the rule for beaver crews; the unlock price is the gate.
  - **Unlock.** `ReactorBotWorkplaceUnlocks` keeps its own saved singleton.
    - Cost: 3 x the native Bot cost. Without one: 3 x (science + 1,000), e.g. District Center 3,000, Restaurant 6,000, Steel Mill 33,000, Hospital 153,000, Launch Pad 603,000, Nuclear Reactor 3,003,000.
    - Flow: the native unlock flow (InstantUnlock key, native dialog box, native locked tooltip) with "Reactor Bots" texts.
  - **District default.** `ReactorBotDistrictDefault` holds a saved flag with the native default set to Bot.
  - **Light.** Green while set to Reactor Bots (priority 30). The earlier "any working Reactor Bot" rule is no longer needed, since only Reactor Bots work there.
- **Harmony** (`ReactorBotWorkplacePatches.cs`, editor-guarded, every target and member checked at mod start):
  1. `WorkerTypeToggle.Initialize` postfix: the third button in the entity panel and the batch control window.
  2. `DistrictCenterFragment.InitializeFragment` postfix: the third button for the district default.
  3. `UnemployedWorkers.UpdateUnemployedState` prefix: Reactor Bots stay out of the native hiring pool.
  4. `PriorityOrderedWorkplaces.GetHighestPriorityUnderstaffedWorkplace` and `GetLowestPriorityStaffedWorkplace` postfixes: native skips Reactor Bot workplaces.
  5. `WorkplaceWorkerType.SetWorkerTypeToDistrict` prefix and postfix: the district's Reactor Bot default.
- **Files:**
  - `src/DeepFrontier/Scripts/SuperBots/`: `ReactorBotWorkplace.cs`, `ReactorBotStaffing.cs`, `ReactorBotWorkplaceUnlocks.cs`, `ReactorBotWorkplacePatches.cs`, and `ReactorBotWorkplaceLight.cs` (reworked);
  - `Scripts/Workforce/SpecialistWorkplace.cs` and `WorkforcePanels.cs` (hand-over and panel texts);
  - `tools/reactor_bot_workplaces.json`, `tools/reactor_bot_workplace_content.mjs` and `tools/validate_reactor_bot_workplaces.mjs`, replacing the `reactor_bot_workplace_light*` trio;
  - `Data/Configurations/DeepFrontierReactorBotWorkplaces.blueprint.json` (replaces `DeepFrontierReactorBotColors`), and six `enUS.csv` rows.
  - `ReactorBot.cs`, `ReactorBotConfigurator.cs` and `super_bots.json` are untouched. New components subscribe to native events in `InitializeEntity`, per the coordinator's rule; the validator checks this.
- **Checks:**
  - **Compile.** `tools/compile-check`: 0 errors.
  - **Generation.** Canonical generation in the mirror changes exactly the singleton (renamed) and the six localization rows.
  - **Validator.** `validate_reactor_bot_workplaces.mjs` passes. It covers the catalog, the generated data, the unlock rule over 70 Iron Teeth workplaces, the runtime (decorators, bindings, light rule, staffing, specialist hand-over, localization keys, no event subscription in `Awake`, the guarded Harmony file) and the native contract decompiled from the plugin assemblies. It fails on seven mutations: light priority 20, the specialist hand-over removed, a subscription in `Awake`, the guard removed, an unknown localization key, multiplier 0, and a stale `DeepFrontierReactorBotColors` file.
  - **Mono harness.** A scratch host in the game's Mono 6.13 against the shipped DLLs ran `ReactorBotWorkplacePatches.Apply`: verification passed, the six methods patched with every parameter injection resolved, the second call was a no-op, and the patched `PriorityOrderedWorkplaces` ran on a native instance. It caught a cast bug first: `SortedList` values implement `IList<T>`, not `IList`.
  - **validate_all.** `-Only` reactor_bot_workplaces, super_bots, workforce_risks, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, automation_content, vertical_slice_content, bot_upkeep, building_descriptions and load_order: 10/10. Full `validate_all -SkipBlender`: 68 of 70 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only).
- **In game, to check (not run; the user was playing):**
  1. Load a save with Reactor Bots (dev mode can finish a Reactor bot recipe at the Bot Assembler).
  2. **Toggle.** Select a Smelter: the toggle reads Beaver | Bot | Reactor Bot (green bot icon, locked).
     - Hover the lock: "Unlock this workplace for Reactor Bots." with the cost (3x its bot unlock, 3,000 for a Smelter).
     - Click it: the confirmation dialog, then science is deducted and the button is active.
     - Reactor Bots arrive, ordinary bots leave, and the windows glow neon green while they work.
  3. **Bot workplaces.** Set another workplace to Bot: only ordinary bots come. A Reactor Bot that was working at a bot workplace in the save leaves it.
  4. **Beaver-only building.** Set a Restaurant (6,000) to Reactor Bots: Reactor Bots serve. Switch it back to Beaver: beavers return, and Bot stays disabled.
  5. **Specialist building.** Set the Nuclear Reactor or Steel Mill to Reactor Bots: the specialist panel says Reactor Bots work there and the "no specialists" status clears. Switch back: specialists return.
  6. **District Center.** Set its default to Reactor Bot, then place a Smelter (Reactor Bots unlocked) and a Gear Workshop (not unlocked): the Smelter starts as Reactor Bots, the Gear Workshop as Bot where its bots are unlocked, otherwise Beaver.
  7. **Batch control.** The workplace tab shows three buttons that work; the hotkey cycles them.
  8. **Saves.** Save and reload: the modes, unlocks, crews and light persist. Load the save without Deep Frontier: those workplaces are bot workplaces.
  9. **Copy settings** from a Reactor Bot workplace to another of the same kind carries the mode.

## Neon green workplace windows while Reactor Bots work; worker-type study (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, bot-light agent; mirror `botwork` on HEAD cb8fb87 + this change; compile-checked; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "We have blue windows when the bots are doing stuff, we should make it neon green when nuclear bots do things." Also asked what a third worker-type option (Reactor Bots, furthest right) would take: a study, not an implementation (`docs/worker-types-study.md`).
- **Native mechanism (decompiled 1.1.2.8, `Timberborn.WorkSystemUI`, `Timberborn.Illumination`):**
  - A workplace whose blueprint has `WorkplaceIlluminatorSpec` gets `WorkplaceIlluminator`. Its `Illuminator` toggle is on while `Workplace.AnyWorkerHasJobRunning()`, checked every tick.
  - It also gets `WorkerTypeIlluminator`, a priority-20 `IlluminatorColorizer` that sets `BotColors.BotIlluminationColor` (`Configurations/BotColors` `BotColorId` -> `IlluminationColor.Bot` 0.16/0.59/0.80) while `WorkerTypeHelper.IsBotWorkerType(WorkplaceWorkerType.WorkerType)`.
  - `Illuminator.UpdateColor` shows the highest-priority colour. The other colorizers: `DefaultIlluminatorColor` 10, `TubeIlluminator` 40, and the player's `CustomizableIlluminator` 100.
  - Blue therefore comes from the workplace's worker-type setting. Only bots can work at a Bot workplace: `Workplace.AssignWorker` throws on a worker-type mismatch. 34 of the 37 generated Deep Frontier workplaces carry `WorkplaceIlluminatorSpec` (not the Grocery Store, Restaurant or Hospital).
- **Rule:**
  - Green while at least one Reactor Bot assigned to the workplace has its job running: the native lights-on test (`Worker.JobRunning`) narrowed to Reactor Bots. Otherwise the native colour, so bot blue while only ordinary bots work.
  - A mixed crew is green while a Reactor Bot is among those working. Beaver workplaces are never affected.
  - A player-set light colour (priority 100) still wins.
- **Runtime (`src/DeepFrontier/Scripts/SuperBots/ReactorBotWorkplaceLight.cs`, no Harmony):**
  - `ReactorBotWorkplaceLight` is decorated on native `WorkplaceIlluminator`, beside `WorkerTypeIlluminator`, by its own `[Context("Game")]` configurator. `ReactorBotConfigurator.cs` and `ReactorBot.cs` are untouched.
  - It creates a priority-30 colorizer on the workplace's `Illuminator`.
  - It follows `Workplace.WorkerAssigned`/`WorkerUnassigned`, keeping each assigned worker's `ReactorBot` (every bot has one, beavers do not).
  - Each tick it sets or clears the colour on a change of "any assigned `ReactorBot.IsReactor` with `Worker.JobRunning`". It implements `ILateTickable`, so it ticks after the entity's `WorkplaceIlluminator` (`TickableEntityLifecycleManager` orders late tickables last) and the tick that turns the lights on also sets the colour.
  - Nothing is saved: on load, `Worker.InitializeEntity` re-employs through `AssignWorker` after every entity's `Awake`, so the list rebuilds.
  - `ReactorBotColors` reads the singleton `FrontierReactorBotColorsSpec {WorkplaceColorId}` the way native `BotColors` reads `BotColorsSpec`, and throws if the colour is not generated (native `FindColorById` only warns).
- **Data (`tools/reactor_bot_workplace_light.json`, written by `tools/reactor_bot_workplace_light_content.mjs`, run by canonical generation after the Reactor Bots step):**
  - `Data/IlluminationColors/IlluminationColor.DeepFrontierReactorBotWorkplace` is neon green #39FF14 (0.224/1.0/0.078). It is not a player preset (no `IlluminationPresetSpec`), like the eye colour.
  - `Data/Configurations/DeepFrontierReactorBotColors` holds the singleton.
  - Canonical generation in the mirror changes exactly these two files.
- **Checks (mirror `botwork`, HEAD cb8fb87 + this change):**
  - `tools/compile-check`: 0 errors (3 earlier warnings).
  - New `validate_reactor_bot_workplace_light.mjs`, covering:
    - the catalog: neon green by hue, saturation and value, distinct from every vanilla colour and the Reactor Bot eye colour;
    - both generated files, a single singleton and a unique colour id;
    - the generator wiring;
    - the runtime decorator, late tick, `IsReactor && JobRunning` test, bindings and no Harmony;
    - the native contract, decompiled from the project's plugin assemblies: the priorities 20 and 100, highest-priority-wins, `AnyWorkerHasJobRunning`, the three `WorkSystemUIConfigurator` decorators, the late-tick order and the `AssignWorker` type check. The priority must lie strictly between the bot blue and the custom colour.
  - The validator fails on priority 20 and on priority 100, on a missing `ILateTickable`, on an "any assigned" test without `JobRunning`, on the vanilla Green colour, on a blue colour, on an edited data file, and on unwired generation.
  - `validate_all -Only` reactor_bot_workplace_light, super_bots, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, automation_content, vertical_slice_content, bot_upkeep, workforce_risks: 8/8. Full `validate_all -SkipBlender`: 68 of 70 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` is report-only).
- **In game, to check (not run; the user was playing):**
  1. Load a save with Reactor Bots, or make one: dev mode, finish a Reactor bot recipe at the Bot Assembler or a retrofit.
  2. Set a lit workplace (for example a Lumber Mill or Smelter) to bots and let only ordinary bots work it: the windows glow blue.
  3. Let a Reactor Bot work there: the windows turn neon green within a tick of it starting work. They go back to blue when it leaves and ordinary bots keep working, and off when nobody works.
  4. Give the building a custom light colour: the custom colour shows whether or not Reactor Bots work there.
  5. Save and reload while green: still green once work resumes.
  6. A beaver workplace keeps its warm light.
- **Worker-type study:** `docs/worker-types-study.md`. The game registers worker types by data (`WorkerTypeSpec`), but the toggle (`WorkerTypeToggle.Initialize`) builds exactly two buttons, and a worker's type comes from its character template, so a Reactor Bot is always "Bot".
  - **Recommended Option 1:** a saved Deep Frontier "Reactor Bots only" setting on Bot workplaces, staffed like specialist workplaces (`SpecialistStaffing` plus the existing assigner registration prefixes), with a third slider button added by a postfix on `WorkerTypeToggle.Initialize`. About 2-3 days, medium risk, no save change.
  - Options 2 (a native "ReactorBot" workplace type plus an `AssignWorker` patch) and 3 (Reactor Bots as their own worker type) are documented with what breaks.
  - Three decisions for the user are listed there.

## Fisher Flag: fresh fish from their own flag; the Gatherer Flag's fishing mode removed (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, fisher agent; mirror `fisher` on HEAD + this change; compile-checked and full validate_all in the mirror; not run in game yet)

- **Player:** "One farm says it needs fresh fish to produce juvenile fish. The other says that it needs juvenile fish to produce fresh fish. The mod doesn't explain how to get them anywhere ... None of them catch either type of fish." **User:** "DO NOT just randomly put extra instructions somewhere ... make a fisher flag, that acts like the gatherer flag but is specifically for catching fish. The beaver will get the 'nothing to do in range' notification if the water is contaminated, too low, etc."
- **Design.** `FisherFlag.IronTeeth` (Food, ToolOrder 11, 0 science, 20 Plank + 10 Log from the material rule on an authored 10 Log + 5 Plank, 1 worker, 1x1x2, F-mirrorable) is the vanilla Iron Teeth Gatherer Flag blueprint (workplace, terrain range, 20-slot output inventory, idle seat, Gatherer outfit, lamp, construction base) with `FisherFlagSpec` in place of `GathererFlagSpec`/`YieldRemovingBuildingSpec`, plus `AmbientAnimationSpec` for the swinging banner (`tools/fishery_building_content.mjs` `authorFisherFlag`; rule data `tools/aquaculture.json` `fisherFlag`). Menu: Gatherer Flag (0), Fisher Flag (0), Farmhouse (0) ... (`validate_tool_order.mjs --print`). The names and descriptions carry the chain: Fisher Flag "Employs a fisher who catches fresh fish from the shore, where the water is clean and deep enough."; Fish Hatchery "Breeds juvenile fish from fresh fish. ..."; Fishery Works "Raises juvenile fish into fresh fish, or presses fresh fish into fish oil." No panel, notice or instruction text.
- **Runtime (`src/DeepFrontier/Scripts/FisherFlag.cs`, no Harmony).** `FisherFlagConfigurator` gives `FisherFlagSpec` the Gatherer Flag's native decorators (decompiled 1.1.2.8 `GatheringConfigurator` and `GatheringUIConfigurator`: `BuildingWithTerrainRange`, `SimpleOutputInventoryFragmentEnabler`, `AutoEmptiable`, `Emptiable`, `HaulCandidate`, `SimpleOutputInventoryHaulBehaviorProvider`, and the workplace behaviors RemoveUnwantedStock, **FisherWorkplaceBehavior**, EmptyOutput, EmptyInventories, Labor, WaitInsideIdly), and every `Worker` gets `FisherCatchExecutor`. `FisherFlag` is the output inventory's `IAllowedGoodProvider` (FreshFish only). It keeps the old shore rule unchanged: a dry bank in the native `BuildingTerrainRange`, water one layer down at least 0.5 deep, badwater share at most 0.01, and a surface from 1.25 below the bank to 0.1 above it. It adds the pumps' radiation rule (`IRadiationService.IsColumnContaminated`, the detection limit), which the old mode lacked. A catch takes 4 base working hours times the native working-speed multiplier; the fish then goes into the beaver's hands with a capacity reservation, and native carrying delivers it.
- **"Nothing to do in range" (native).** With no suitable bank, `FisherWorkplaceBehavior.Decide` calls `NothingToDoInRangeStatus.ActivateStatus()` and releases the worker; a found bank calls `DeactivateStatus()`. Evidence (decompiled 1.1.2.8): `WorkSystemConfigurator` decorates every `Workplace` with `NothingToDoInRangeStatus` (loc `Status.Yielding.NothingToDoInRange`, "Nothing to do in range", a normal status with alert and floating icon). The Gatherer Flag reaches the same toggle through `YieldStatus.UpdateStatus` (`NoYielderInRange` -> `ActivateStatus`), and `LumberjackFlagStatusDeactivator` calls `DeactivateStatus`. The status clears itself when the building is blocked or loses its last worker.
- **Gatherer Flag:** no longer patched. `Data/Buildings/Food/GathererFlag/GathererFlag.IronTeeth.blueprint.json` is deleted; `ShoreFishing.cs`, `ShoreFishingPanel.cs` and the three `GatherWorkplaceBehavior`/allowed-good Harmony patches in `DeepFrontierModStarter` are removed.
- **Saves:** no migration code.
  - A Gatherer Flag saved in fishing mode gathers again: the entity loader is pull-based, so its `DeepFrontierShoreFishing` entry is never read.
  - FreshFish in its stock is native unwanted stock (`Inventory.LimitedAmount` 0), which `RemoveUnwantedStockWorkplaceBehavior` carries off.
  - A beaver saved in `ShoreCatchExecutor` resumes with no executor: `BehaviorManager.DeserializeExecutor` finds no `IExecutor` of that name and skips `Load`. A fish in its hands loses its reservation in `GoodReserver.ResolveLoadedReservations` (a native warning) and is emptied natively.
  - Not yet loaded in game.
- **Model.** `tools/asset-pipeline/fisher_flag_geometry.py` and `build_fisher_flag.py` write `src/DeepFrontier/Art/Source/FisherFlag-ReferenceStudy.blend` (textures external, 0.7 MB) and the official Timbermesh exports in `outputs/civic-integration/Data/Buildings/Food/FisherFlag/` (finished 796 triangles, against the vanilla flag's 466; construction stage 102).
  - Parts: a roped plank landing, a net with red floats on the back ropes, two rods, a tub of catch, a crate, and a pole with a lantern and a blue swallowtail banner with a fish on both faces. The banner swings in a 60-frame loop (`#FisherFlag.Banner`); the idle seat is `#Slot#Seat0`.
  - Materials: BaseWood_DarkBrown, Details and WindowsAtlas (Iron Teeth), one atlas island per face.
  - Icon: `src/DeepFrontier/Art/Icons/FisherFlag.svg`, the vanilla Gatherer Flag pennant with a fish, rendered by `create_restaurant_icons.mjs`.
  - Reviews: `docs/art/reviews/2026-11-02-fisher-flag/` and `docs/art/reviews/2026-10-21-icons/civic-aquaculture.png`.
- **Registries:**
  - building catalog;
  - triangle budget (`FisherFlag`, civic small, ceiling 796/102);
  - connection faces (none) and roof hardpoints (excluded: a walked floor);
  - material mix and cost context;
  - building sounds (GathererFlag selection, no loop);
  - automation (pausable; `FisherFlagSpec` operational: `CanWork` needs `BlockableObject.IsUnblocked`);
  - progression `MAP_SOURCES`: FreshFish via the Fisher Flag.
- **Map checks:** `prepare_deep_frontier_map.py` reads `FisherFlagSpec` from the Fisher Flag blueprint. The item is now "Fisher Flag banks (first FreshFish)"; 7 built-in maps keep it as `playerWork`, with no notice. `map_loop_requirements.json`, the variant reasons, `test_prepare_deep_frontier_map.py` (22/22) and docs/maps.md follow.
- **Acceptance fixtures (not run):**

## Industrial bot recipe from the Reactor Bot retrofit parts (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, industrial-bot agent; mirror `indbot` on HEAD 22e5c8d + this change; not built or run in game)

- **User decision** on the charging gap left open in the entry below (Industrial bots ran from about 12,000 science, the Reactor Charging Bay unlocks at 400,000): the Industrial bot recipe uses the retrofit parts.
- **Recipe** (`tools/workforce_risks.json` botAssembly): 1 Reinforced Bot Frame + 1 Reactor Power Cell + 4 Bot Limb + 1 Bot Head -> one Reactor Bot in 24 h (was 1 Bot Chassis + 4 Bot Limb + 1 Bot Head + 1 Power Module + 2 Steel). Nothing is counted twice: the frame is 8 Steel + 4 Alloy + 1 Machine Core + 1 Bot Chassis, so the chassis and steel go; the cell is 1 Enriched Fuel + 2 Power Module + 2 Electronics + 2 Cryocoolant, so the power module goes; limbs and head are in neither part and stay. It equals the retrofit path (an ordinary bot plus both parts) less the ordinary bot's chassis, three times faster than the vanilla 72 h. Recipe icon: the reactor power cell (was the power module). 4 ingredients (was 5).
- **Unlock:** the recipe runs once the Uranium Processing Plant (300,000) makes cells (frames from the Precision Machine Works, 12,000); the bay follows at 400,000, the same step as the retrofit. `audit_progression` warns `recipe-input-unlocks-later` (Reinforced Bot Frame 12,000, Reactor Power Cell 300,000), as it warned for the power module before; no failure.
- **Generation order:** bot upkeep runs before `super_bot_content.mjs` writes the parts, so `planBotUpkeep` accepts ingredients and an icon that are Reactor Bot parts declared in `tools/super_bots.json` (`reactorPartIds`); `validate_bot_upkeep` requires them in the generated data. A misspelt ingredient fails both.
- **Text:** Bot Assembler description (175 characters): "Assembles bots from a chassis, four limbs and a head. Industrial bots take a reinforced bot frame and a reactor power cell instead of the chassis and come out as Reactor Bots." The 24 h shows in the recipe panel.
- **Data:** canonical generation, `material_costs --write`, `lategame_costs --check` (exit 0), regeneration and `material_costs --check` at a fixed point: only `Recipe.IndustrialBot`, the description row and the IndustrialBot entry of `tools/material_cost_context.json` change; no construction cost changes. `docs/item-use-audit.md` regenerated: Reinforced Bot Frame and Reactor Power Cell 2 -> 4 uses (minimum met -> preferred range; the audit counts a product-less recipe both as "Bot assembly" and as "Disposal"), Power Module 12 -> 10.
- **Checks:** full `validate_all -SkipBlender` 67 of 69 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` report-only); `audit_progression` 0 failures; `validate_bot_upkeep`, `validate_super_bots` pass. No C# change.
- **In game, to check:** the Industrial bot recipe at the Bot Assembler lists the frame, the cell, four limbs and a head with the cell icon; haulers deliver both parts; a finished one is a Reactor Bot (the entry below); a queued retrofit and the assembler competing for the same parts both complete.

## Industrial bots are Reactor Bots; radioactive-yellow eyes; Reactor Charging Bay beside the Charging Station (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, industrial-bot agent; mirror `indbot` on HEAD 624e503 + this change; compile-checked; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "Even when industrial bot is selected as the recipe, it still doesn't make them as industrial bots"; asked for steel plating and radioactive-yellow eyes instead of blue; wanted the upgraded bot charger right next to the vanilla Charging Station in Science. Coordinator, for the user: "we already specced out what we want for the buffs": an Industrial bot is the specced Reactor Bot upgrade (Reactor Bots, 2026-10-29: x5 load, x4 speed, x10 lifespan, Energy replaced by reactor charge, coolant and steel plating), not a new variant; the retrofit stays for ordinary bots.
- **Cause:** native `BotFactory` has one `BotSpec` template and `BotManufactory` spawns it on every `ProductionFinished`, whatever the recipe, so the Industrial bot recipe made an ordinary bot.
- **Native hook (decompiled 1.1.2.8, no Harmony):** `BotManufactory` subscribes to `Manufactory.ProductionFinished` in `InitializeEntity`; `FinishProduction` raises it with `CurrentRecipe` still the finished recipe; the handler's `BotFactory.Create` -> `EntityService.Instantiate` instantiates and initializes the bot (PreInitialize, Initialize, PostInitialize, PostLoad) before returning, with a `CharacterBirthInit` whose `EventToPost` is a `BotManufacturedEvent`. New `Scripts/SuperBots/ReactorBotAssembly.cs`: `ReactorBotAssembler` (decorated on the new `FrontierReactorBotAssemblySpec {RecipeId}`, which `bot_upkeep_content.mjs` adds to the Bot Assembler's same-path patch) subscribes in `Awake`, so it runs before the native handler, and announces a Reactor Bot birth to the `ReactorBotAssemblyLine` singleton when the finished recipe is `IndustrialBot`; `ReactorBot.InitializeEntity` takes it only for a bot whose birth init carries `BotManufacturedEvent`, sets the Reactor state and resets the reactor needs to full (the state a finished retrofit leaves); a second subscription in `PostInitializeEntity` (after the native one) throws if the bot did not take it, and a second announcement before that throws. Bots from the vanilla recipe, the dev bot tool or another mod's factory stay ordinary.
- **Saves:** no new saved data: the bot saves the existing `DeepFrontier.ReactorBot` `State` = `Reactor`. Bots already in a save keep their state (ordinary stays ordinary; an industrial bot made before this change stays ordinary and can be retrofitted).
- **Look (decided from source):** the native beaver variant (`CharacterTextureSetter`) picks a random texture pack per instantiation and saves nothing, and would need new textures; per-bot looks stay on the Reactor Bot route. Eyes and lamps: native `MaterialColorer` lighting colour `IlluminationColor.DeepFrontierReactorBot` 1.0/0.52/0.12 (orange) -> 0.64/0.69/0.06, the softened radiation yellow `RadiationMaterials.WaterBaseColorB` (vanilla bot blue 0.16/0.59/0.80). Steel plating: the existing `CharacterTint` 0.62/0.66/0.72, now shown only while the plating need is favourable (dropped while unplated, restored when the bot takes steel or alloy); colour-named bots keep the native colour.
- **Texts:** Bot Assembler description (154 characters): "Assembles bots from a chassis, four limbs and a head. Industrial bots add a power module and steel, take a third of the time and come out as Reactor Bots." Recipe name "Industrial bot" unchanged; the bot panel shows the Reactor Bot rows.
- **Menu:** Reactor Charging Bay `toolGroup` Metal/46 (in Nuclear fuel) -> Science/75, between the vanilla Charging Station (70) and Control Tower (80). New `tool_groups.json` `upgrades` {ReactorChargingBay: ChargingStation.IronTeeth}: `tool_group_content.mjs` checks the pair (generated building, not listed in a group, same tool group as its base); `validate_tool_order.mjs` requires the upgrade immediately right of its base and leaves it out of the science rules, which every other tool keeps (without the declaration the same placement fails with 2 ascending errors and 2 inversions; ToolOrder 85 fails "must stand immediately right of ChargingStation"). Science now reads Inventor, Numbercruncher, Bot Part Factory, Bot Assembler, Charging Station, Reactor Charging Bay, Control Tower, Grease Factory, Technical School, Materials Laboratory, Archive Excavation.
- **Checks:** `validate_bot_upkeep` checks the spec record, decorator and hook; `validate_super_bots` checks the eye colour against `RadiationMaterials.WaterBaseColorB` (fails on the old orange) and the plated-only tint; `automation_content.json` declares the new spec (operational: no progress, no bot, while paused). Canonical generation in the mirror changes exactly the Bot Assembler patch, the charging bay's tool group and order, the light colour and the assembler description row. Targeted `validate_all -Only` super_bots, bot_upkeep, tool_groups, tool_order, automation_content, component_lifecycle, editor_guards, workforce_risks, building_descriptions, audit-progression, vertical_slice_content 11/11; full `validate_all -SkipBlender` 67 of 69 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and passes on this commit's private index; `audit-item-uses` report-only); `tools/compile-check` 0 errors (3 earlier warnings).
- **Open, balance (for the user):** the Industrial bot recipe can run from about 12,000 science (Precision Machine Works parts and power modules, Steel Mill steel; coolant from the Water Treatment Plant, 1,800), but the Reactor Charging Bay unlocks at 400,000 and Reactor Bots cannot use the vanilla station, so an early Industrial bot works for its first 6.9 days of charge and then stops until a bay exists. Options: make the recipe take the retrofit parts (Reinforced Bot Frame + Reactor Power Cell, which puts it at the Uranium Processing Plant's 300,000, like the retrofit), or move the bay earlier (it burns enriched fuel and is built from those parts).
- **In game, to check:** finish an Industrial bot at the Bot Assembler (yellow eyes, steel tint, panel "Reactor Bot: ...", Energy gone, reactor charge/coolant/plating full); a vanilla-recipe bot stays ordinary with blue eyes; save and reload keeps both; let plating run out (tint off, back after it takes steel); the Science menu order.

## Radioactive tide settings on the new-game screen (Claude Code session, 2026-11-02, tide-mode agent; mirror `tidemode` on HEAD 8c4d6ab; compile-checked, data path run in a harness against the shipped DLLs; not built or run in game)

- **User request:** "Can we add the radioactive tide settings into the game mode selection when starting a new game (easy, normal and hard mode). I can set stuff like badtide percent chance and first bad tide and drought duration etc.... we should add radioactive tide settings into there as well."
- **Native mechanism (decompiled 1.1.2.8, details and [S] evidence in `docs/radiation.md` "Settings per game").** Game modes are blueprints `NewGameModes/GameMode.Easy|Normal|Hard` with a fixed `GameModeSpec` record; "Customize" fills `CustomNewGameModeController` fields from the selected preset and `TryGetValidatedGameMode` builds a new `GameModeSpec` (and enables Start); `NewGameModePanel.StartNewGame` passes the spec in `GameSceneParameters` to the Game scene, where `BadtideWeather`/`DroughtWeather` read it once and save their own values (older saves without them take the default mode).
- **Added.** Each native game mode gets a `RadioactiveTideGameModeSpec` (blueprint overlay `Data/NewGameModes`, written by `tools/radioactive_tide_content.mjs`): Easy on, 24 cycles without (from cycle 25), 15%, 2-4 days; Normal on, 14 (from 15), 25%, 3-6 days (the 0.8.3 defaults); Hard on, 9 (from 10), 35%, 5-10 days. The custom mode shows "Radioactive tides", "Cycles without radioactive tide", "Radioactive tide chance" and "Radioactive tide duration" after the badtide rows: the native badtide rows of a second copy of the native panel view, moved, renamed and relabelled (`RadioactiveTideNewGameSettings`, main menu; no UXML or USS added). One Harmony postfix on `CustomNewGameModeController.TryGetValidatedGameMode` (`RadioactiveTideNewGamePatch`, verified at mod start by `RadioactiveTideNewGameNative.Verify`, applied with or without Moddable Weathers) disables Start while the rows are invalid and gives the custom spec a blueprint that also holds the tide values; presets need no code.
- **Stored per game:** singleton `DeepFrontier.RadioactiveTideSettings` (`RadioactiveTideGameSettings`), read once from the new game's mode. Saves from 0.8.3 and earlier take the values the player had in the former mod settings (native PlayerPrefs keys `ModSetting.DeepFrontier.RadioactiveTideSettings.*`; unchanged keys = the old defaults = Normal). Selection now uses "cycles without" (tides from cycle N + 1), like the native badtide.
- **Mod Settings:** the eMka "Radioactive tide" page (`RadioactiveTideSettings.cs`, all five options) is removed; nothing else in Deep Frontier uses eMka.ModSettings. It stays in the manifest's RequiredMods and the Workshop list until you decide to drop it.
- **Moddable Weathers:** its per-save tide settings stay the only tide settings with it (the vanilla settings are not bound), now seeded from the same source (the save's vanilla tide settings, else the new game's mode, else the former mod settings) instead of the global mod settings. A save played with Moddable Weathers and then without it takes the former mod settings.
- **Checks (mirror on 8c4d6ab + this change, files that differ from HEAD pinned to HEAD):** canonical generation (Data differs from HEAD only by `Localizations/enUS.csv` and the three `NewGameModes` overlays); compile checks main, Moddable Weathers, Sluice is Back!, Power Lines: 0 errors; the new files also compile against the shipped non-publicized Managed assemblies (public API only, plus the verified `NewGameModePanel._root` read); `validate_radioactive_tide_content.mjs --native <decompiled 1.1.2.8> --moddable-weathers <installed 11.0.0>` passes, and fails on a mutated preset, a re-added `RadioactiveTideSettings.cs` and a missing docs row; `validate_all -Only` radioactive_tide_content, editor_guards, mod_dependencies, deep_frontier_manifest, vertical_slice_content, building_descriptions, load_order, component_lifecycle, contamination_consumers, contamination_sensor pass; a .NET harness against the shipped 1.1.2.8 DLLs passes 21 cases (custom-mode copy keeps every native value and carries the tide spec, preset lookup, a dropped blueprint and a mode without tide settings fail, new-game/saved/old-save values, invalid saved or old values fail, switched-off values unchecked).
- **Not verified in game (the user was in the game).** Steps: (1) main menu, New game, any map, the mode screen: Easy/Normal/Hard as before; select Hard, Customize: below the badtide rows "Radioactive tides" is on with 9 / 35% / 5 to 10 days; Normal gives 14 / 25% / 3 to 6; Easy 24 / 15% / 2 to 4; unticking hides the three rows. (2) Set the shortest tide above the longest: both fields turn red and Start is disabled; fix it and Start enables. (3) Start a custom game with 1 cycle without, 100%, 1 to 1 days; `Player.log` shows "Radioactive tide settings: on, from cycle 2, 100% per cycle, 1-1 days (the new game's mode)"; with dev mode, "Jump to next season" until cycle 2: a 1-day radioactive tide. Save, reload: the log says "(this save)" with the same values. (4) Start a Hard preset game: the log shows "from cycle 10, 35% per cycle, 5-10 days". (5) Load a 0.8.3 save: the log shows the former mod settings (the old defaults unless changed) and the game plays as before. (6) Mod Settings: Deep Frontier has no "Radioactive tide" page. (7) With Moddable Weathers enabled: a new custom game's Weather Settings dialog shows the tide with "Earliest cycle" = cycles without + 1 and the chosen chance and days; `Player.log` "Moddable Weathers radioactive tide settings seeded: ...".

## Restaurant kitchen: no batch starts before the next recipe is chosen (Claude Code session, 2026-11-01, kitchen agent; mirror `kitchen` on HEAD 9710a62, compile-checked; build 249 (verified, 779 hashes) run in game: Restaurant fixture run and reload, all-buildings `kitchen-249` batch 1)

- **Reported:** all-buildings b242/b244 "Restaurant automatic kitchen: FermentedCassava cooked twice, FermentedSoybean never finished"; the Restaurant fixture (b244) stopped on "Automatic kitchen did not alternate between the two stocked recipes by need: FermentedCassava,FermentedCassava". These were two causes.
- **Bug, fixed (fixture, and real play with two or more cooks): the chooser lost a race to the cooks.** Evidence: `restaurant-run244.log` "KITCHEN finished FermentedCassava ... switches 1", then at once "NATIVE COOKING started FermentedCassava", and the second finish still "switches 1", while three staff ran ProduceExecutor and SweetBerryCake (stock 0/32) was neediest. Source (decompiled 1.1.2.8): `Manufactory.IncreaseProductionProgress` calls `FinishProduction` -> `ResetProductionProgress` -> `ProductionFinished` inside the finishing cook's `ProduceExecutor.Tick`; the next `IncreaseProductionProgress` by any cook consumes the ingredients and adds progress; `TickableBucketService` ticks entities in 128 buckets by entity-id hash. A cook in a bucket between the finisher and the Restaurant started the finished recipe again before `RestaurantKitchenChooser.Tick`, whose mid-batch rule (progress > 0 and products fit) then kept it. With one worker the Restaurant always ticks between two of its ticks, which is why b239 (one worker, no guests) passed. Fix (`Restaurant.cs`): the chooser is a native `IManufactoryLimiter` (Manufactory collects them in Awake); `ProductionFinished` sets a pending choice and `ProductionEfficiency()` is 0 while it is pending, so `IsReadyToProduce` is false for every cook until Tick has chosen among cookable recipes; the pending flag is saved (`ChoicePending`, absent when false; older saves load unchanged).
- **All-buildings: the one-worker kitchen starved, not the chooser.** b242/b244 picked FermentedSoybean correctly after FermentedCassava (2 switches, reason LowestStock; 6 of 12 Soybean consumed) but its single worker served the pad's other crews from hour 2.4 to 10 (status lines show RestaurantWorkBehavior; Berries 32 -> 24, FermentedCassava 10 -> 3). `Restaurant.IsCook` makes half the staff (rounded down) cooks, so a lone worker serves first and cooks only between guests (design since 2026-10). The harness now crews the Restaurant with two (one cook, one waiter who also cooks between guests, so the multi-cook case is exercised); waves are packed by crew size.
- **Static check:** `validate_restaurant_content.mjs`: the chooser implements IManufactoryLimiter with efficiency 0 while a choice is pending, ProductionFinished sets it, only the cookable choice in Tick clears it, and it is saved and loaded. Fails on HEAD's `Restaurant.cs` ("A finished kitchen batch must stop production ..."), passes now. `validate_all -Only component_lifecycle,editor_guards,priority_statuses,restaurant_content,restaurant_grid,inventory_enable_guard` 6/6; `tools/compile-check` builds with 0 warnings.
- **In game (build 249):** Restaurant fixture (`restaurant-run249.log`): "KITCHEN finished FermentedCassava ... switches 1", "KITCHEN finished SweetBerryCake ... reason LowestStock; switches 2", "PASS automatic kitchen cooked Fermented Cassava and Sweet Berry Cake, alternating by need", "PASS with the cake's Corn used up the kitchen kept cooking Fermented Cassava", table service and four cakes passed, "SAVED dining fixture" (`Restaurant acceptance - verified` re-made). Reload (`restaurant-reload249.log`): every check passed, "RELOAD AND INTERRUPTION CHECKS COMPLETE", no exceptions. All-buildings `kitchen-249`: "Restaurant worker assigned: assigned 2 of 2", "automatic kitchen cooks every stocked recipe: batches [FermentedCassava,FermentedSoybean,FermentedCassava]" PASS and "keeps cooking without one recipe's ingredients" PASS.
- **Not verified:** a save written while a choice is pending (a one-tick window); the rest of the all-buildings run beyond the Restaurant's wave (see the run's JSON when present).
- **Design note for the user:** a one-worker Restaurant in a busy district never cooks (serving comes first); unchanged.

- **Naming (vanilla 1.1 style):** 78 building names to Title Case with "x" sizes ("Cement Kiln", "Concrete Platform 3x3 (3 high)", "Steel Deck 3x3 on Legs (1 high)"; vanilla "Overhang 2x1"); goods in sentence case with real plurals (`create_frontier_expansion_content.mjs` GOOD_DISPLAY_NAMES is now [singular, plural] for every good and throws on a missing one; radiation, super-bot and catalog goods likewise), so recipe names read "Machine cores", "Rocket airframes". "Radioactive Zombie" -> "Radioactive zombie". Tool group "Concrete scaffolds" -> "Concrete platforms" (name only, `tool_groups.json`). Recipes that share a product say what they use ("Water from badwater", "Water from contaminated water" (DecontaminateWater showed only "Water"), "Chemicals from concentrated waste", "Enriched fuel from radioactive waste"); the construction recipes show their product ("Dirt", "Bricks", was "Grind brick earth" ...). The 39 Recipe.*.DisplayName rows no recipe read are no longer written (only RECIPE_NAMES rows).
- **Flavor:** one line per building (`BUILDING_TEXT`, `construction_materials.json` and `structural_content.json` `flavor` fields, the generators throw on a missing one); size variants of one family share theirs. Replaced the 19-fold "The colony's next industrial step ...", 11-fold "The next age begins ...", 12-family "Room for the next layer ..." and the reactor/vault shared line.
- **Wording:** no "×" anywhere (panels say "4 times faster", the Deep Well slider "2x"); hatchery placement "pen tile(s)" instead of "marked working cell(s)"; hospital "Treatment speed: N% (set by its staffing)"; meltdown dialog "turn radioactive ... pumps there stop" (not "contaminated", which is another good); US spelling (color, humor, labeled, fueled, travelers, distills). C#: turbine panel "Turbine / Flow limit / Flow through the turbine / Water level at the intake / at the outlet / Drop" and states "No drop: ..." / "No water flowing through" ("Dry intake" kept: two acceptance checks read it); Restaurant "Needs power"; Grocery Store title; shore fishing "1 fresh fish per N working hours, plus walking ...".
- **Validator (`validate_building_descriptions.mjs`, contract extended, nothing dropped):** building names in Title Case, goods in sentence case with both rows, a flavor line belongs to one building family, every Recipe.* row is some recipe's DisplayLocKey, and on every row no "×", no internal terms (blueprint, spec, stockpile, tick, understructure, authored, placeholder), US spelling, no retired terms ("Deep Processor", "brine", "valve set to Inlet/Outlet"). It fails 275 times on HEAD's localization and passes on the new one. `validate_deep_well_content.mjs` checks the drought rule says "2 times", no Deep Well row uses "×" and the description names the steel drill bits.
- **Result:** 98 building descriptions, mean 109.6 -> 115.9 characters (limit 120), longest 200 (Nuclear Reactor). Changed rows by category: building descriptions 50, display names 78, flavors 67, goods 67, recipes 13 (+39 dead rows removed), narrative 15, rocket program 10, panels/statuses/notifications 39.
- **Not changed:** MapSetup rows and `map_setup.json` (map agent); docs that quote old names (developer docs).
- **In game, to check:** hover a piped building and a Timber Pipes valve: the tooltip's Fill/Extract match the valve panel; recipe lists show the plural names; the Hydro Turbine panel; a Reactor Bot panel line.

## Second shrink checked in game: mines and quarries reachable, old saves keep their buildings, flipped buildings keep their ground (Claude Code session, 2026-11-01, in-game verification agent; mirror `verify` on HEAD 89fd754 then 88e9b71 plus these changes, committed as 23b50f7 and the following commit; builds 239 (HEAD 89fd754), 240, 242, 243, 244 (= 23b50f7), 245-247 (da5d47c; 247 with the probe fixes of 25569be) verified, 779 hashes; final installed build 248 = e85ebef (verified, not run in game); 241 = 0.8.1 (1364df0, mirror `old081`) for the baseline; run in game)

## Small maps: Diorama, Cliffside and Beaverome finish the game; no setup notices (Claude Code session, 2026-11-01, small-maps agent; generated and validated in mirror `smallmaps`; first commit 88e9b71 on 0ee9eea, follow-up on 23b50f7; not built or run in game)

- **User:** "We should try to get the places like Diorama, Cliffside and Beaverome working, since our buildings are smaller and the players can use platforms etc." Follow-ups the same day: "Don't modify the base game." (the dry-land vanilla Underground Ruins of 88e9b71 removed); "The user can drain the lakes if they want."; "No new game notice. People know how to play." (gaps ordinary play fixes are met and silent).
- **Missing on 0ee9eea** (`builtin`): Diorama: Limestone Quarry, Nuclear Reactor and Cryogenic Liquefaction Plant sites. Cliffside: Nuclear Reactor site, shore fishing banks. Beaverome: both quarry sites, Uranium Processing Plant site, Nuclear Reactor site, renewable scrap (both Underground Ruins on lake beds). The quarry and plant reasons were measured with the 8x6 quarries and the reserved Launch Pad site, both gone with the second shrink: with the 5x5 quarries Beaverome has 239 tree-free seam positions, and the planner found the Diorama and Beaverome plants at once. Deep ore, stone budgets, water and caches were already met on all three.
- **Raised flat sites** (`platformBlocks` on flat-rule sites, `platform_fill` in `tools/prepare_deep_frontier_map.py`): the building stands at the site level on stackable supports; footprint, margins and door approach may lie below that level, never above; the declared count must equal the 1x1x1 blocks (Concrete Block, 12 Concrete + 3 Sandstone, 1,200 science) under every foundation block that needs matter below. Source evidence (1.1.2.8, decompiled `Timberborn.BlockSystem.MatterBelowValidator`): `GroundOrStackable => AtGroundLevel || StackableBelow`; every flat-rule building's layer 0 is `GroundOrStackable`/`Any` only (reactor 137 + 17, plants, Deep Processor, Steel Mill, Deep Drill, Archive Excavation, power plants); the mod's only validator looking under a foundation (`RoofHardpointValidator`) refuses hardpoints, not platforms. Understructure, pit and map-edge sites reject the key (deposits stay real ground); a `Ground` foundation on a raised site is a site problem.
- **`playerWork`** (variant key beside `unmet`, `resolve_declared_gaps`): loop items the saved map lacks but ordinary play provides. Validated like `unmet` (must equal the failing items, never both lists); the item then counts as met with `"ready": false` and the reason in the evidence (`declaredPlayerWork`), and the generator makes no notice line for it. Every former `unmet` item on every built-in map moved there, with the play in its reason: fishing banks (Canyon, Cliffside, Craters, Hollows, Mountain Range, Nomads, Waterfalls: dam), farmland, trees, pines (Pillars, Pressure, Thousand Islands: irrigate, plant, bridge), hydro site (Oasis: Aquifer Drills on level-6/7 aquifers above level-2 ground; Pillars: seeps on levels 11-13 and an aquifer on level 7 above level-0 ground), Beaverome's renewable scrap (drain). **No built-in map declares `unmet`, so no built-in map shows a setup notice**; the notice mechanism stays for items no play can provide. `tools/map_setup.json` `unmetText` lost the fishing, farmland, tree and pine lines.
- **Flooded ruins in the evidence:** `startResources.undergroundRuins[].underWater` records depth, contamination, lake size, the lake's source strength and the shore route; the renewable-scrap detail lists them. Beaverome: (117, 41, 3) under 6.22 of clean water, 11,197-cell lake, source strength 33.5, shore at route 0; (149, 137, 0) under 8.58 of badwater, 3,682 cells, shore at route 69. Vanilla Beaverome's description: its builders "drained seven mighty craters".
- **Reverted from 88e9b71:** `tools/map_setup.json` `vanillaMapObjects`, the vanilla-template support in the catalog and in `map_setup_content.mjs` (back to 0ee9eea), the placed-ruins coverage and the Beaverome Underground Ruins placement. A negative test now checks that a vanilla map object is refused.
- **Diorama** (17 placements, 22 sites, nothing declared): reactor (28, 0, 3) Cw270 on 1 platform block (one level-2 cell among 137 supported), water 1, 98 trees; Deep Processor (43, 16, 6), Steel Mill (37, 4, 4) and Radioactive Waste Export (46, 22, 6) moved off that pocket; new Limestone Seam (45, 10, 6) with its quarry; Cryogenic Liquefaction Plant (2, 9, 2).
- **Cliffside** (17 placements, 23 sites, player work: fishing banks): reactor flat at (15, 24, 4) Cw90, four open sides, water 1, no trees; the Deep Ore Vein at (21, 10, 4) and its Deep Mine moved to the level-8 bench (60, 25, 8). Fishing: a dam raising the 0.53-deep bay by about 0.25 brings its banks (1.48 above the water) within the ShoreFishing reach of 1.25.
- **Beaverome** (17 placements, 22 sites, player work: renewable scrap): Limestone Seam (83, 79, 12) and Sandstone Seam (95, 45, 13) with their quarries; Uranium Processing Plant (42, 162, 12); reactor (37, 26, 11) Cw180 on 39 platform blocks (28 raised cells, at most 3 layers), water 0.
- **Player text:** generated `DeepFrontierMapSetup.blueprint.json` has empty `UnmetLocKeys` on all 19 maps and enUS has no `DeepFrontier.MapSetup.Unmet.*` rows; Workshop Maps paragraph, `docs/maps.md` (status, Data and validation, Small maps, per-map table now listing player work, notes, in-game steps 5-7, negative tests) and CHANGELOG updated.
- **Tests:** `tools/test_prepare_deep_frontier_map.py` 22/22 faults caught: five new (platform count, raised below terrain, platforms under a quarry, vanilla map object, player work for a met item) and four repaired cases that had gone stale with the second shrink and the finite-ore rule (they crashed on the removed Restaurant site, so the file had not run since).
- **Checks (mirror, final change on 23b50f7):** `prepare_deep_frontier_map.py builtin` 19 built-in maps OK, none small, none with unmet items; canonical generation reaches a fixed point (only the map-setup blueprint and enUS rows differ from HEAD); full `validate_all -SkipBlender` 66 of 68 pass (`no-game-assets` needs git and runs on the commit's private index instead, where it passes; `audit-item-uses` report-only).
- **In game, still to test:** new games on Diorama, Cliffside and Beaverome (Player.log `placed` counts 17/17/17) and on a former notice map such as Canyon or Pressure: no Deep Frontier dialog; a Nuclear Reactor on one Concrete Block at Diorama's (28, 0, 3) Cw270 (native preview should accept it with the block and refuse it without).

Brief: `outputs/compaction/BRIEF.md` plus the second-shrink brief (one layer per floor, footprint inside the old one with the same origin, costs held).

All on target; no one-tile deviation.

Door x 2.5 -> 1.5. Its four freed corner ground cells (2026-10-22) are now under the tube's footing and stair, so `lategame_freed_cells.json` lists none inside.

## Second shrink: civic A (Claude Code session, 2026-11-01, civic-A shrink agent with three forks; Grocery Store, Restaurant, Fishery Works, Fish Hatchery, Materials Laboratory; each building generated and validated in a mirror of HEAD + that building, then committed; not built or run in game)

- **User:** a YouTuber's first look at 0.8.x called the Grocery Store and Restaurant "massive" ("why is grocery store so massive?"); "we need to shrink the buildings down even more". Brief: `SHRINK2-BRIEF.md` with `outputs/compaction/BRIEF.md` (open flat roofs, roof variety, no clutter on buildable cells, one layer per floor).
- **Grocery Store 9x6x5 -> 5x4x3** (004683f, scene fix 6f5c803): a one-storey timber market hall (eaves 1.25) on 4x3 under the teal gable roof with the lit clerestory monitor; the low stall wing on the left (fish steps, cassava crocks and soybean jars, the basket sign), the front pent over the packaged-food steps and corn crates, the striped awning over the algae stall. The balcony, stair bay, upper market room, cross-gable and dormers are gone. All 18 stock-stage nodes and 4 animated parts kept. Door x 4.5 on y 2 -> x 3.5 on y 1 (a door in the last tile of the 4-tile front would cut its corner post): doorstep (4,2) -> (3,1), Entrance and clerk cell (4,1) -> (3,0). Cells 132 -> 41; connection faces 15 -> 8; roof decks 3 -> 0 (pitched roof); triangles 18,304 -> 8,453. GroceryAcceptance/GroceryContinuityAcceptance: the market layout is the door walk only (compiled, not run).
- **Restaurant 9x7x5 -> 6x5x3** (36bfe5c): ground floor the vestibule and a 5x3 hall whose east end is the kitchen (bake oven in its back wall); layer 1 the 3x3 dining room, the front dining balcony (3 tables) and the side balcony over the kitchen (1 table); layer 2 open flat roof decks (timber on cast slabs, no rails) with 2 open-air front tables and 1 side table, and a one-layer stairhead. **7 tables and 14 seats kept** (capacity 14); seats 0.20 and tabletops 0.475 over the decks, lower diners' heads 0.06 under the deck beams. The carved sign (same node, same sway) hangs from a bracket arm off the front-right deck post. Same origin, ground door (1.5,1,0) and Entrance (1,0,0); the dining-room door moved to x 2.5; 3 door links (was 4). Connection faces 226 -> 127; platformable roof cells 41 -> 20; triangles 12,234 -> 8,201. validate_restaurant_grid/content follow the new contract; RestaurantAcceptance expects two deck layers (compiled, not run).
- **Fishery Works 11x8x4 -> 6x5x3** (8f5e665): a one-storey hall with a flat timber deck roof at layer 1 and the upper hall under the long teal gabled roof; the wheel, header basin, cascade, two basins, crane, net and sign kept; the tanks (pipe inlet) under the upper hall. The water wing moved to the left (x 0..1) so the door on tile 3 is clear of the hall's corner post. Same origin, door cell (3,0,0) and Entrance (3,-1,0). Pipe port (4,6,0) -> (3,4,0). Cells 164 -> 60; connection faces 75 -> 54; platformable deck cells 15 -> 9. Art moved to `fishery_works_geometry.py` (grid coordinates); the old `civic_isometric_geometry.fishery()` is unused.
- **Fish Hatchery 7x7x3 -> 5x5x3** (b97497d, repaired by d443a0b): headhouse 3 wide under its flat timber roof (door (1.5,1,1) unchanged), open pump canopy 2x2, pens 2x2 at x 0..2 and 3..5 (were 2x3), centre dock, header tank on a bank cell. Pen work cells (1,4,0),(0,4,0),(3,4,0),(4,4,0) (first is still the contamination sample); deck piles 4; pipe port (6,2,1) -> (4,2,1). Same origin, BaseZ, door and Entrance. Cells 59 -> 35; connection faces 79 -> 47; deck cells 15 -> 8. It keeps its water sites; dynamite outside the new footprint dropped (Cliffside 3, Diorama 3, Hollows 1, Terraces alternative 2). b97497d was staged from a mirror on an older HEAD and rolled back other agents' rows in shared files; d443a0b restored them (checked key by key: only FishHatchery keys differ from 92757d5).
- **Materials Laboratory 10x8x5 -> 5x4x3** (1dc7947): the two sawtooth sheds on a 3x3 cast base, the entrance annex in front with the ground door whose flat roof is the open terrace, the press and the scaled instrument tower on 2x2 plinths in the right column; the records office and the service bay are gone. Same 3 animated parts (flywheel radius .73 -> .50). Same origin, door cell (1,0,0) and Entrance (1,-1,0). Connection faces 154 -> 36; platformable roof cells 18 -> 3; triangles 15,684 -> 10,192. ResearchAcceptance reads the deck layer from the stackable roof (compiled, not run).
- **Costs unchanged** on all five. The material rule recomposes a building's cost from its re-measured mix, so the new `tools/material_mix_basis.json` keeps the pre-shrink material families for pricing (`material_costs.mjs` `pricedFamilies`; the measured `building_material_mix.json` row still drives the stale-mix audit). Power, workers, recipes, capacities and science unchanged.
- **Maps:** at 5x4 / 6x5 the Grocery, Restaurant, Fishery and Laboratory no longer need flat sites: their rows left all 20 variants, the Beaverome, Cliffside and Diorama "No ready space for" rows dropped them, `smallMap.maxArea` was re-derived at each commit (3188 at HEAD), docs/maps.md rows removed (this commit).
- **Saves:** every building keeps its origin and frees the tiles it no longer uses. Grocery: re-lay its path one tile left to the new Entrance; paths on the old balcony float. Restaurant: paths on the old rear, outer side and upper decks and the roof terrace float; flipped, the Entrance moves 3 tiles (width 9 -> 6). Fishery: basins and wheel now on the other side; reconnect a pipe at (3,4,0). Hatchery: pens one row nearer the bank (may be shallow or dry on steep shores); reconnect a pipe at (4,2,1). Laboratory: flipped, the Entrance moves 5 tiles; paths on the old terrace rows float. Shafts on removed walls disconnect.
- **Checks (per building, mirror HEAD + that building):** strict z-fight 0 visible (frames 1/31/61/91), hidden geometry 0 culled faces exposed, scenes unpacked and normalized (0.3 MB; 004683f first committed a packed 62 MB Grocery scene, fixed in 6f5c803, the blob stays in history); canonical generation; validate_all exports-* of the family, model-bindings, triangle-budget, animations, building_entrances, connection_faces, roof_decks, flat_roof_platforms, freed_cells, roof_hardpoints, vertical_slice_content, construction_materials, status_icon_anchors, map_setup, map-setup-terrain, audit-progression, audit-building-assets (plus restaurant_content/grid, fishery walks, sources-research); compile check 0 errors. Review sheets: `docs/art/reviews/2026-11-01-shrink2-civic/`.
- **Open:** nothing seen in game (placement, walking, table service, pens, deck paths, save/reload of old placements); the C# fixtures were updated and compiled, not run; Restaurant workbook/verify scripts and `test_hatchery_structure.mjs` (stale since 2026-10-26) not updated; untracked files under `docs/art/reviews/` were deleted several times during the pass by some other process.

- **Sandstone Pit 5x4x3 / Limestone Pit 5x3x3 -> 3x3x3** (`pit_isometric_geometry.py`, 2dde8ad): one plan: store room on column 1, bay with the stone bin on column 0, one flat roof (sheets / steel plate), jib hoist on column 2 (post and winch at the back, basket over the yard floor in front, sandstone, or over open ground in the middle row, limestone). ## Second shrink: industry (Claude Code session, 2026-11-01, industry shrink agent; Steel Mill (ae35d1c), then Deep Ore Processor, Precision Machine Works, Food Packaging Works, Chemical Works and Archive Excavation together with the industry cost hold (8d9b538); generated and validated in mirror `shrink-ind2` on HEAD + those buildings; not built or run in game)

- **Why:** the user, after a YouTuber called several buildings "massive": "we need to shrink the buildings down even more". Rules: `outputs/compaction/BRIEF.md` plus the second-pass brief (targets per building, one layer per floor, same origin and entrance, costs, recipes, power and workers unchanged).
- **Checks (mirror `shrink-ind2` on 8d9b538):** canonical generation reproduces the committed data (no file differs); full `validate_all` 71 of 73 pass, `audit-item-uses` report-only, `no-game-assets` cannot run without git in a mirror and passes in the repo; compile check 0 errors. Per building: strict z-fight 0 visible (frames 1/31/61/91), hidden-geometry audit 0 culled faces exposed, grid audit with no floor misses, every new blueprint cell inside the allowed set (old occupied cells, or above old non-stackable blocks). Renders are Cycles on the RTX 2060 (OptiX); no Workbench renders were made.
- **Save rule used for every building here** (source: `BlockObject.AddToServiceAfterLoad` deletes a loaded object that is no longer valid, with a loading notice; two objects claiming one cell make the second one invalid): each new blueprint only claims cells the old one claimed, or cells above an old non-stackable block with nothing between (where nothing of the player's can stand). Then a saved building can never lose its place to a path, platform or building the player put next to or on top of it.
- **Steel Mill 8x6x8 -> 5x4x6** (`lategame_SteelMill.py`): a two-storey pulpit and melt hall (tiles 0..2 x 0..3, door front) and a painted-sheet canopy over the rolling line (front right: reheat furnace, roller table, three 0.8-scale two-high stands) and the teeming bay (tilting ladle over ingot moulds, tap hole on the hall wall) form one roof deck on layer 2; the four-layer furnace bay fills the back row with the one-tile electrode penthouse (three electrodes in a row) and the flue rising from the ground through its roof. Removed: the stand motors and pinion boxes, the tall melt shop over the middle rows (only the back row rises above layer 2 now) and the long fume duct over the roof. The 13 animated parts keep their names (the pulpit beacon now sits on the penthouse cap). Triangles 9,130 -> 6,708.
  - Numbers (HEAD -> now): solid cells 139 -> 54; shaft faces 138 -> 80 (no pipe ports: no liquid recipe); measured flat faces 211 -> 102; platformable roof cells 49 -> 18 (every flat roof cell); roof hardpoints 10 -> 0; one smoke source.
  - Cost: back to the pre-shrink amounts (MetalBlock 265, DeepAlloy 135, Concrete 870, Gear 255) since the industry group commit: the pre-shrink material families are held in `tools/material_mix_basis.json` (another agent's mechanism, added after ae35d1c), undoing the recomposition ae35d1c reported.
  - Maps: 5 < `largeFootprint`, so the mill is listed in `FLAT_SITE_TEMPLATES` (`prepare_deep_frontier_map.py`, `docs/maps.md`): it is the only maker of Flux, High-Grade Steel and Alloy, the deep-industry stage needs it, and its 20 map sites stay valid (same origin and door approach). `smallMap.maxArea` re-derived.
  - Saves: same origin, door tile and Entrance (1,-1,0); a flipped mill's entrance moves 3 tiles (local x 6 -> 3). Every new block is a block the old mill occupied (checked cell by cell against the HEAD blueprint), so no path, platform or building a player put beside or on the old mill overlaps it and nothing is deleted at load for overlap. The roofs inside the new footprint sit exactly where the old roof decks were (layer 2 over the front three rows, layer 4 at back-row tiles 0, 3 and 4), so things standing on them keep their support; things on the old roof decks outside the new footprint, and steel columns on its 10 old roof hardpoints, lose their support and the game removes them on load with its loading notice. Shafts on removed walls disconnect.
  - Review: `outputs/compaction/SteelMill-shrink2.png`.
